- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: European review for medical and pharmacological sciences
- 作者: Biglia A, Morandi V, Bartoletti A, Bozzola M, Iannone C, Cartia CS, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct
- DOI: 10.26355/eurrev_202610_38012
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42961161/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Cryofibrinogenemia (CF) is a rare systemic condition characterized by reversible precipitation of circulating plasma components such as fibrinogen, fibrin, or fibronectin at cold temperatures that resuspend upon rewarming to body temperature. It is crucial to consider the possibility of CF in the differential diagnosis of patients with purpura or ischemic skin lesions. PLEX may serve as an effective salvage treatment for severe CF manifestations, such as refractory skin ulcers or limb digital ischemia that do not respond to conventional immunosuppressive treatment. Unfortunately, randomized controlled trials are lacking, and no guidelines exist. Our current knowledge is based on case reports and case series.
CASE SERIES: We report a case series of three patients treated at IRCCS Policlinico San Matteo, Pavia, Italy, who presented with different clinical manifestations of CF refractory to conventional immunosuppressive therapies and were successfully treated with plasmapheresis (PLEX).
CONCLUSIONS: Management of CF includes glucocorticoids, whereas azathioprine, rituximab, cyclophosphamide, anticoagulants, and intravenous fibrinolysis have been reported to be effective in more severe cases. The proposed maintenance therapy for moderate disease is corticosteroids and low-dose aspirin or stanozolol. Treatment of refractory symptomatic CF remains challenging. Plasmapheresis (PLEX) has been reported in a few cases in the literature as a useful treatment for CF. The contribution of our cases highlights the importance of considering CF in the differential diagnosis among patients with purpura or ischemic skin lesions and PLEX as an effective salvage treatment for severe CF manifestations, such as refractory skin ulcers or limb/digital ischemia that do not respond to conventional immunosuppressive treatment.

- DOI: 10.7759/cureus.117146

- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

Although hepatitis A virus infection is mostly self-limited, it can rarely cause acute fulminant liver failure, especially in resource-limited settings where emergency liver transplant is unavailable. We present the case of a 14-year-old girl diagnosed with acute fulminant hepatitis A complicated by severe hepatic encephalopathy (Glasgow Coma Scale (GCS)-2T/10), acute kidney injury (AKI), and ventilator-associated pneumonia (VAP). On admission, the patient required mechanical ventilation and intensive supportive care. Due to rapidly worsening coagulopathy (international normalized ratio (INR): 4.39) and profound hyperammonaemia (391 µmol/L), she underwent three sessions of therapeutic plasma exchange (TPE) alongside targeted broad-spectrum antimicrobial therapy. Marked clinical and metabolic improvement was observed post-TPE, culminating in the full recovery of hepatic and renal function and total resolution of her encephalopathy. She was successfully discharged with normal organ function. This case emphasizes the critical value of early diagnosis and aggressive multidisciplinary management and highlights the effective role of TPE as a temporary metabolic bridge during the critical phase of illness to spontaneous recovery in selected cases of paediatric fulminant hepatic failure (FHF), enabling survival without liver transplantation even amidst severe multiorgan dysfunction.

### [A] Recurrent Immune-Mediated Thrombotic Thrombocytopenic Purpura in the Setting of Suspected Sjögren's Syndrome.

- DOI: 10.7759/cureus.117022
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42948852/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

A 70-year-old African American female with a past history of relapsing/remitting thrombotic thrombocytopenic purpura (TTP), hypertensive retinopathy, and breast cancer was evaluated in the rheumatology clinic for a possible autoimmune rheumatic disease. Earlier, she had followed up with a community rheumatologist who diagnosed her with systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE) and Sjögren syndrome (SS). She was being treated with hydroxychloroquine, but this was discontinued after her hospitalization for a TTP flare. Investigations revealed the presence of anti-nuclear antibodies (ANA) and anti-SSA antibodies. Anti-double-stranded DNA (dsDNA) and anti-Smith antibodies were not present. Her main complaints were dry eyes and dry mouth, and there were no rashes, mouth sores, alopecia, serositis or polyarthritis. Although she had a positive ANA, the available clinical and serologic documentation did not establish sufficient additional criteria to fulfill the 2019 European Alliance of Associations for Rheumatology/American College of Rheumatology (EULAR/ACR) classification criteria for SLE. Her relapsing TTP was treated with multiple courses of rituximab and plasmapheresis with ADAMTS13 level monitoring. Mycophenolate mofetil (MMF) was added as a steroid-sparing immunosuppressive agent in the setting of recurrent immune-mediated TTP, ongoing corticosteroid exposure, and concomitant autoimmune features suggestive of Sjögren disease. Thereafter, the patient remained stable without another documented TTP relapse during follow-up. This temporal association does not establish that MMF prevented TTP relapse. This case report emphasizes an interesting association between suspected Sjögren disease and recurrent TTP and highlights the diagnostic and therapeutic challenges of managing recurrent TTP in the setting of systemic autoimmune features.

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND/OBJECTIVES: Phototherapy is an effective first-line treatment for neonatal jaundice. Previous studies have demonstrated a linear dose-dependent relationship between irradiance and bilirubin reduction during intensive phototherapy up to approximately 55 μW/cm2/nm, although others have suggested that the therapeutic effect plateaus with higher irradiance. However, the efficacy and safety of treatment at these irradiance levels remain unclear. By focusing on newborns with severe jaundice close to the exchange transfusion threshold, we aimed to examine whether the dose-dependent association between phototherapy irradiance and bilirubin reduction is maintained at irradiance levels above 55 μW/cm2/nm.
METHODS: Here, phototherapy delivered at estimated irradiance levels >55 μW/cm2/nm was defined as ultra-high-irradiance phototherapy. We retrospectively reviewed 92 neonates with severe jaundice admitted to a tertiary neonatal intensive care unit between 2017 and 2024. Severe jaundice was defined as total serum bilirubin levels above or within 2.5 mg/dL of the exchange transfusion threshold. We evaluated the dependence of the bilirubin reduction rate on estimated irradiance and other clinical variables.
RESULTS: Only one neonate required exchange transfusion. In multivariable analysis, the bilirubin reduction rate was associated with estimated irradiance (p < 0.001) and total serum bilirubin at phototherapy initiation (p < 0.001). Bilirubin reduction was dose-dependent on estimated irradiance up to 148 μW/cm2/nm (0.31 mg/dL/h per 100 μW/cm2/nm). Transient hyperthermia occurred in 12% of infants.
CONCLUSIONS: Higher estimated irradiance was associated with greater bilirubin reduction up to 148 μW/cm2/nm. These findings support further prospective evaluation of optimized phototherapy irradiance in severe neonatal jaundice.

### [A] Acute Management of Refractory Bullous Pemphigoid Using Plasma Exchange and Granulocyte-Monocyte-Apheresis: A Case Report.

- PMID: 42923975
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Independent plasma exchange/blood exchange research interest; not disease-domain relevance
- 优先级依据: A: plasma exchange/plasmapheresis/blood exchange/immunoadsorption
- 主题: 换血/血浆置换疗法
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: Case reports in dermatology
- 作者: Ciobanu G, Reichinger E, Saulite I, Cozzio A, Arn N
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.1159/cde/abvag011
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42923975/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

INTRODUCTION: Bullous pemphigoid (BP) is a chronic autoimmune blistering disorder, characterized by subepidermal blister formation and intense pruritus, predominantly affecting older adults. Its pathogenesis is complex and only partially understood, involving both humoral and cellular immune mechanisms. Current treatment strategies rely primarily on topical or systemic corticosteroids, depending on disease extent. However, the long-term use of corticosteroids is often constrained by substantial toxicity, prompting the use of steroid-sparing agents such as methotrexate, rituximab, and other immunomodulatory therapies. Nevertheless, the management of BP remains challenging, particularly in refractory cases, due to frequent relapses and treatment-associated adverse events.
CASE PRESENTATION: We report the case of a 67-year-old woman with long-standing refractory BP and multiple relapses despite various therapeutic interventions, including dupilumab. Due to persistent disease activity, therapeutic plasma exchange was performed, resulting in marked clinical improvement and allowing subsequent maintenance therapy with intravenous immunoglobulin (IVIg). Six months later, however, she developed a severe flare with progressive blistering that remained refractory to high-dose systemic corticosteroids. Following repeat plasma exchange and subsequent granulocyte-monocyte apheresis (GMA), a marked clinical response was observed, with achievement of sustained clinical remission.
CONCLUSION: In this case of severe refractory BP, sequential extracorporeal therapies were temporally associated with disease control. Plasma exchange followed by IVIg was associated with an initial clinical response, whereas repeat plasma exchange and subsequent GMA coincided with remission during a later inflammatory relapse. Although a causal relationship cannot be established from a single case, these observations suggest that tailored extracorporeal approaches including GMA may represent a valuable therapeutic option in selected patients with severe, treatment-refractory BP and warrant further investigation.

### [A] A case report of plasma exchange in imidacloprid poisoning: A promising therapeutic approach for acute toxicity.

- PMID: 42953826
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory

- 期刊: Asian journal of transfusion science
- 作者: Yadav A, Gupta GN, Agarwal P, Sheemhar A
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.4103/ajts.ajts_76_23
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42953826/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

Imidacloprid, a neonicotinoid insecticide, is commonly used in agricultural and urban settings for pest control. However, its toxic effects on both target and nontarget organisms, including humans, have raised concerns. Imidacloprid poisoning can result in a wide range of symptoms, including neurological, gastrointestinal, and cardiovascular manifestations, and may even be fatal in severe cases. In recent years, plasma exchange has emerged as a potential therapeutic approach in cases of imidacloprid poisoning, with promising outcomes reported in a few published case reports. This article reviews the role of plasma exchange in imidacloprid poisoning, highlighting its mechanism of action, clinical indications, technical aspects, and potential benefits. The article also discusses the current evidence supporting the use of plasma exchange in imidacloprid poisoning, including relevant case reports and studies. While plasma exchange appears to be a promising adjunctive therapy in managing imidacloprid poisoning, further research and clinical experience are needed to establish its effectiveness and safety. This article emphasizes the importance of early recognition and management of imidacloprid poisoning, including considering plasma exchange as a potential treatment modality in severe cases.

### [A] Immune cells in cerebral small vessel disease: Emerging roles and potential targeting therapies.

- PMID: 41622493
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Independent plasma exchange/blood exchange research interest; not disease-domain relevance
- 优先级依据: A: plasma exchange/plasmapheresis/blood exchange/immunoadsorption
- 主题: 换血/血浆置换疗法
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: Neural regeneration research
- 作者: Zhan Y, Wang Y, Bai M, Feng J, Wei J, Wang J
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 01
- DOI: 10.4103/NRR.NRR-D-25-00858
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41622493/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-01

#### 摘要

Cerebral small vessel disease is an age-related condition that severely affects the quality of life of older adults; however, there are currently no definitive treatments or preventive measures. Cells from both the peripheral and central immune systems significantly impact the development of cerebral small vessel disease. By analyzing the effects of different immune cells on brain dysfunction associated with this disease, we aim to explore the various mechanisms through which autoimmune and acquired immune cells in peripheral circulation and the central nervous system contribute to disease development. Additionally, we seek to identify potential therapeutic modalities targeting these immune cells. In innate immunity, treatment targeting different monocyte loci includes four main modalities: cytokines (CSF1R, MIF, P2X7, CX3CR1, and CCL2), signaling pathways (DAP12/TREM, PI3K/AKT, and Wnt/β-catenin), tissue engineering (mitochondrial transplantation and exosomes), and traditional Chinese medicines. Treatment targeting different dendritic cell loci encompasses two modalities: signaling pathway (cGAS/STING/NF-κB) and tissue engineering (tolerogenic dendritic cells and engineered probiotics). For natural killer cells, treatment targeting different loci includes two modalities: tissue engineering (natural killer cell-based immunotherapies) and traditional Chinese medicines ( Tongxinluo capsule). In adaptive immunity, treatment targeting different T cell loci includes four modalities: cytokines (S1PR and RXR), signaling pathway (Nrf2/GPX4 and Notch-ITGB1), tissue engineering (elimination of senescent T cells, monoclonal antibodies targeting amyloid-beta protofibrils, nanovaccines, and nanomedicines), and traditional Chinese medicines (traditional herbal remedies and microneedle therapy). Treatment targeting different B cell loci includes three modalities: monoclonal antibodies (targeting CD49d and CD20), purine glycoside analogs (Cladribine), and plasma exchange. This review explores the relationship between nerve regeneration, immune cells, and cerebral small vessel disease. The disease is characterized by the crucial role played by signaling and interactions between immune cells and components of the neurovascular unit, which is a functional complex composed of neurons, glial cells, and microvessels that regulate inflammatory responses and tissue repair. The central nervous system lacks intrinsic regenerative capacity, and currently, there is no effective method to fully restore its function. The primary focus of current research is on utilizing tissue engineering and regenerative medicine to create environments that facilitate cell proliferation and tissue regeneration. Hydrogels, induced pluripotent stem cells, exosomes, regulatory T cell transplants, and gene modification are key areas of focus in neural regeneration research. In the future, immune rejuvenation and other emerging therapies may offer potential treatment strategies for cerebral small vessel disease. This review analyzes the functions of various immune cells in cerebral small vessel disease, explores the relationship between nerve regeneration and these immune cells, and discusses potential therapeutic avenues that target immune cells for future treatments and neural regeneration related to cerebral small vessel disease.

### [A] Clinical characteristics and outcomes of 144 critically ill children receiving therapeutic plasma exchange in a pediatric intensive care unit: a single-center retrospective study.

- PMID: 42964209
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Independent plasma exchange/blood exchange research interest; not disease-domain relevance
- 优先级依据: A: plasma exchange/plasmapheresis/blood exchange/immunoadsorption
- 主题: 换血/血浆置换疗法
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 独立科研兴趣: Plasma exchange / blood exchange
- 期刊: Frontiers in pediatrics
- 作者: Chen Z, Wei Q, Liu J, Zeng C, Zhao W
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.3389/fped.2026.1955886
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42964209/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-01

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Therapeutic plasma exchange (TPE) is increasingly used in critically ill children, yet pediatric data remain scarce. This study aimed to characterize the clinical features, indications, complications, and outcomes of children receiving TPE in a pediatric intensive care unit (PICU).
METHODS: We retrospectively enrolled 144 children who underwent TPE in the PICU of Zhujiang Hospital, Southern Medical University, between January 2017 and December 2021. Demographic data, diagnoses, TPE indications (categorized according to the 2019 American Society for Apheresis [ASFA] guidelines), complications, and outcomes were collected. Multivariate logistic regression was performed to identify risk factors for mortality.
RESULTS: The 144 children (median age, 66 months; median Pediatric Risk of Mortality III score, 18) underwent 414 TPE sessions. The leading indications were neurological disorders (29.2%), liver diseases (19.4%), and hematologic disorders (16.7%); 57.6% of patients were treated for ASFA category III indications. The complication rate was 16.9% per session, with filter or circuit clotting the most frequent event (7.2%); all complications resolved after prompt management, and no treatment-related death occurred. Overall PICU mortality was 36.8%, highest in the liver disease group (60.7%) and lowest in the neurological disorder group (16.7%) (P = 0.012). Non-survivors had higher rates of multiple organ dysfunction syndrome (MODS), mechanical ventilation, and vasopressor use than survivors (all P < 0.001). Multivariate analysis identified MODS (odds ratio [OR] = 5.609, 95% confidence interval [CI]: 1.189-26.453) and vasopressor use (OR = 6.551, 95% CI: 1.310-32.768) as independent risk factors for mortality.
CONCLUSIONS: TPE is generally safe in critically ill children when performed in an experienced PICU. MODS and vasopressor requirement independently predicted death. Because no control group was included, treatment efficacy could not be evaluated; our conclusions are limited to the safety and clinical profile of TPE in this population.

#### 摘要

Hypertriglyceridemia-induced acute pancreatitis (HTG-AP) is associated with a more severe clinical course than acute pancreatitis due to other etiologies. Presently, these patients are managed with dietary restrictions, analgesia, lipid-lowering agents, insulin/heparin dual therapy, and supportive care to prevent organ failure. Apart from these conventional therapies, therapeutic plasma exchange (TPE) is another potential treatment that is being performed as it results in rapid reduction in serum triglycerides and inflammatory cytokines, thus, leading to drastic improvements in clinical and laboratory findings and patient outcomes. Here, we report our experience of using TPE for HTG-AP in our patients.

### [A] Role of therapeutic plasma exchange (TPE) in the management of anti-glomerular basement membrane (anti-GBM) disease: A case series from tertiary-care hospital in India.

- PMID: 42953509
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Exploratory

- 期刊: Asian journal of transfusion science
- 作者: Pawar S, Tiwari AK, Golia S, Aggarwal G, Gupta S, Bhardwaj G, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.4103/ajts.ajts_216_24
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42953509/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-01

#### 摘要

INTRODUCTION: Anti-glomerular basement membrane (anti-GBM) disease is an uncommon, autoimmune disease in which IgG antibodies bind to intrinsic antigens distributed along the length of glomerular basement membrane. It is characterized by rapid and progressive loss of renal function. The rationale of management is to suppress renal inflammation and production of autoantibodies (with immunosuppression), as well as removal of circulating pathogenic autoantibodies (with therapeutic plasma exchange [TPE]). However, there is a paucity of data on the role of TPE in the management of anti-GBM cases worldwide.
MATERIALS AND METHODS: Three consecutive anti-GBM patients were admitted with common complaints of high-grade fever, chills, hematuria, and low urine output. In view of decreased renal function and strong suspicion of anti-GBM disease, the treatment protocol was initiated in accordance with the international Kidney Disease Improving Global Outcomes guidelines.
RESULTS: All three patients had crescentic glomerulonephritis (crescents in more >80% glomeruli) on renal biopsy. Management was initiated with TPE and immunosuppression (methylprednisolone) and cytotoxic drug (cyclophosphamide). TPE was done until the anti-GBM antibody titer dropped to <10. Case 1 underwent TPE daily (12 sessions), and case 2 and case 3 underwent 14 and 9 sessions, respectively, of TPE alternating with dialysis. Renal function improved in cases 1 and 2, and routine dialysis was discontinued. Case 3 showed improvement initially until TPE was done; however, the disease progressed to end-stage renal disease, and the patient remains dialysis dependent. None of the patients had adverse reactions during TPE.
CONCLUSION: TPE is an effective and safe conjunct in the treatment of anti-GBM disease as shown by complete reversal in two cases and partial response in the third patient.

### [A] Clostridium perfringens Delta toxin targets CD9 to drive NEC and brain injury.

- PMID: 42951958
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 跨领域顶刊启发
- 来源类型: 顶刊扩展
- 来源说明: Cell/Nature/Science生命科学顶刊
- 期刊: Nature
- 作者: Zhang S, Gong X, Tian S, Yang Y, Tong X, Sheng K, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 28
- DOI: 10.1038/s41586-026-11211-1
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42951958/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-30

#### 摘要

Necrotizing enterocolitis (NEC) is the most devastating gastrointestinal emergency in preterm infants, frequently causing mortality and long-term neurodevelopmental impairment1,2. Although the gut microbiota has been implicated in NEC pathogenesis3-5, the microbial factors driving NEC and the following brain pathology remain unclear. Here we identify Clostridium perfringens pathobionts containing Delta toxin in the gut microbiome of individuals with severe and fatal NEC. Clinically, infected infants show worse outcomes, including aberrant systemic inflammation, severe neurological damage and coagulopathy. We establish a mouse model in which Delta-toxin-positive C. perfringens infection, or Delta toxin exposure alone, recapitulates clinical features of NEC, including intestinal necrosis and acute cerebral injury. Mechanistically, Delta toxin preferentially targets macrophages, potently activating inflammasome signalling and pyroptosis, triggering a rapid inflammatory cascade that compromises blood-brain barrier integrity and drives neuroinflammation. Through a genome-wide CRISPR screen, we identify the tetraspanin CD9 as the host receptor for Delta toxin. Using systemic, myeloid-specific and intestinal-epithelial-specific Cd9-knockout mice, we show that systemic and myeloid Cd9 ablation protects mice against toxin-induced lethality, blood-brain barrier dysfunction and multiorgan injury, whereas epithelial Cd9 deletion shows partial protection, implicating macrophage targeting as a primary driver of C. perfringens CD9-dependent pathogenesis. Furthermore, a neutralizing anti-Delta toxin monoclonal antibody confers protection against disease progression. These findings suggest that a subset of NEC is a specific-toxin-mediated disorder and validate the Delta toxin-CD9 axis as a therapeutic target for this subset.

### [A] Local albumin excess exacerbates Candida albicans-induced inflammasome activation linked with hyperinflammation during vulvovaginal candidiasis.

- PMID: 42957760

- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 29
- DOI: 10.1038/s41467-026-78761-w
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42957760/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

Vulvovaginal candidiasis (VVC) is a mucosal yeast infection, mostly caused by Candida albicans, where symptoms are driven by inflammation. The onset of VVC is instigated by C. albicans pathogenicity, but the underlying causes of disease-driving hyperinflammation remain incompletely resolved. We found that vaginal albumin concentrations are higher in women with recurrent VVC and during experimental VVC in mice. These albumin levels correlated with inflammatory cytokines in vaginal lavages. While the abundance of this serum protein in the vagina can be a hallmark of inflammation-induced vascular permeability, we reveal molecular mechanisms by which albumin can also drive inflammation. Albumin increased NLRP3 inflammasome activation in human macrophages elicited by C. albicans. This was associated with increased resistance of C. albicans to macrophage-mediated clearance in the presence of albumin. Further, albumin increased the expression of fungal secreted aspartic protease 1, which we identified as the effector driving inflammasome activation and IL-1β responses in vitro and in an in vivo VVC model. Moreover, neutrophils also exhibited impaired clearance of C. albicans in the presence of albumin. Collectively, vaginal albumin may not only be a consequence of inflammation of the vaginal mucosa, but also can drive C. albicans-induced hyperinflammatory IL-1β responses that underlie immunopathology in VVC.

### [C] Adma and Ackr3a regulate cardiac lymphatic vessel formation and function during heart development and regeneration.

- PMID: 42946752

- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 17
- DOI: 10.1038/s41467-026-78353-8
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42946752/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-29

#### 摘要

Cardiac lymphatics regulate fluid homeostasis and immune cell clearance. The mechanisms underlying cardiac lymphatic vessel formation remain unclear. We perform spatial transcriptomics on the zebrafish heart at the single-cell level and investigate gene expression patterns in cardiac lymphatic endothelial cells (LECs). adrenomedullin a (adma) is mainly expressed in the epicardium adjacent to cardiac lymphatics. adma mutants develop edema and dilated outflow tracts with mis-patterned and reduced lymphatics, while their ventricular lymphatics are dilated with fewer sprouts, increased lyve1b expression, and altered lymphatic subpopulation composition. Loss of ackr3a, which encodes an Adm decoy receptor, increases lymphatic sprouts. During heart regeneration, adma and ackr3a are expressed in epicardium-derived cells within the wound. Neutrophil clearance is reduced in adma mutants but increased in ackr3a mutants. Our findings uncover the heterogeneity of cardiac LECs and show that Adm and Ackr3 regulate cardiac lymphatic vessel formation in the zebrafish heart.

### [C] Growth factor VEGF-B sustains intestinal Tfh cell stemness to restrict Taurinivorans outgrowth and microbiota-driven obesity.

- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

Autocrine signaling through vascular endothelial growth factor receptor-1 (VEGFR-1) reprograms T cell metabolism upon TCR stimulation. We found that mice bearing conditional deletion of Flt1 (encoding VEGFR-1) in T cells developed spontaneous obesity with advancing age. Analyses of lymphoid and metabolic tissues identified a VEGFR-1+ T follicular helper (Tfh) population in Peyer's patches and mesenteric lymph nodes. Flt1 deletion impaired Tfh fitness and reduced secretory IgA in the gut, resulting in unrestricted expansion of the pathobiont Taurinivorans. Taurinivorans overgrowth depleted host taurine, which drove obesity via suppressed adipocyte lipolysis and thermogenesis. Mechanistically, a VEGFR-1-AKT signaling axis promoted Tfh cell stemness by limiting p53-mediated senescence. This Tfh-microbiota-obesity pathway was also observed in diet-induced obese mice and in humans. Oral administration of IgA reduced Taurinivorans and alleviated adiposity, highlighting the therapeutic potential of this axis. Tfh cell senescence impairs IgA production, disrupting gut microbial homeostasis, with consequences to host metabolic disease.

### [C] The neurobiology of menopause: Implications for brain aging and Alzheimer's risk.

- PMID: 42962664
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance

- 主题: 跨领域顶刊启发
- 来源类型: 顶刊扩展

- 期刊: Neuron
- 作者: Mosconi L
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 30
- DOI: 10.1016/j.neuron.2026.09.015
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42962664/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-01

#### 摘要

A growing body of evidence implicates the menopausal transition as a neurobiological inflection point in women's brain aging and Alzheimer's disease (AD) vulnerability. Menopause is a midlife neuroendocrine transition characterized by a decline in sex steroid hormones and widespread systemic effects. The brain is not a passive recipient of ovarian hormones but an active endocrine-sensing organ. Menopause, however, does not act in isolation: the ovarian-brain axis is established embryonically, activated at puberty, remodeled during pregnancy and postpartum, and reconfigured during the menopausal transition, with implications for later-life brain aging. This review places menopause within a lifespan neuroendocrine framework, examining mechanisms by which the transition may influence the brain, including altered hormone signaling, bioenergetics, synaptic plasticity, neurovascular function, inflammation, and AD-related pathology. It then reviews evidence linking menopausal timing to neurobiological and AD vulnerability, discusses estrogen therapy, and considers menopause as a biologically plausible window for risk stratification and prevention.

### [C] Xijiao Dihuang decoction attenuates microglia/macrophage-mediated inflammatory response in cerebral injury by modulating the PI3K/Akt/HIF-1α/NLRP3 axis.

- PMID: 42642006
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Journal of ethnopharmacology
- 作者: Fang M, Yang J, Gao M, Zhao Y, Wang M, Liang W, et al.
- 发表日期: 2027 Jan 30
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jep.2026.122330
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42642006/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-26

#### 摘要

ETHNOPHARMACOLOGICAL RELEVANCE: Xijiao Dihuang Decoction (XJDH), originating from Beiji Qianjin Yaofang (Thousand Gold Prescriptions for Emergencies) compiled by Sun Simiao during the Tang Dynasty (652 AD), is a classical Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) formula comprising Cornu Bubali (substituted for Rhinoceros horn), Radix Rehmanniae, Paeoniae Radix Rubra, and Cortex Moutan. In TCM clinical practice, XJDH has traditionally been prescribed for "Xue-Re" (Blood-Heat) syndrome characterized by febrile diseases with bleeding tendency, blood stasis and impaired consciousness, a clinical pattern that shares pathophysiological features with the inflammatory cascade and microcirculatory dysfunction in acute ischemic stroke (AIS). However, the molecular mechanism by which XJDH alleviates cerebral injury via the microglia/macrophage inflammatory cascade remains incompletely characterized.
AIM OF THE STUDY: This study aims to elucidate the mechanism by which XJDH ameliorates cerebral injury by mitigating the microglia/macrophage inflammatory cascade, in association with the PI3K/Akt/HIF-1α/NLRP3 axis.
MATERIALS AND METHODS: The middle cerebral artery occlusion (MCAO) model in C57BL/6 J mice and a lipopolysaccharide (LPS)-induced inflammatory model in RAW264.7 and THP-1 cells were established for in vivo and in vitro experiments, respectively. Pathological alterations, inflammatory responses, and the PI3K/Akt/HIF-1α axis were assessed by HE staining, TUNEL assay, immunofluorescence, ELISA, RT-qPCR, and Western blot analyses. The PI3K inhibitor LY294002, the Akt agonist SC79 and the HIF-1α agonist DMOG were employed for pathway verification. Serum from 60 participants (20 controls, 20 AIS, and 20 AIS treated with XJDH) was also analyzed for inflammatory cytokines.
RESULTS: XJDH dose-dependently promoted neurological function, decreased cerebral infarct volume, attenuated histopathological injury, and mitigated cell apoptosis in MCAO. Network pharmacology identified 244 shared targets between XJDH and AIS, with PI3K/Akt and HIF-1 pathways ranking among the top enriched pathways. Molecular docking confirmed favorable binding affinities of key XJDH constituents to PI3K (-8.7 kcal/mol), Akt (-6.0 kcal/mol), and HIF-1α (-7.0 kcal/mol). XJDH significantly enhanced PI3K and Akt phosphorylation levels, downregulated HIF-1α and NLRP3, and shifted microglia/macrophages from an M1-toward an M2-dominant phenotype in MCAO mice. These effects were largely abolished by LY294002 in both RAW264.7 and THP-1 cells. Consistently, Akt activation by SC79 potentiated, whereas HIF-1α stabilization by DMOG reversed, the anti-inflammatory effect of XJDH, supporting the involvement of the Akt-HIF-1α axis.
CONCLUSIONS: XJDH ameliorates cerebral injury in mice in association with the PI3K/Akt/HIF-1α/NLRP3 axis and a shift of microglia/macrophages away from an M1-dominant phenotype; these findings provide preclinical, ethnopharmacology-oriented rationale warranting further validation in neuron-macrophage co-culture systems and, ultimately, randomized clinical evaluation.

### [C] Integrated network analysis, proteomics, and experimental validation reveal the mechanisms underlying the renoprotective effects of salvianolic acid A against diabetic nephropathy.

- PMID: 42537762
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Journal of ethnopharmacology
- 作者: Luo Z, Ma Y
- 发表日期: 2027 Jan 30
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jep.2026.122209
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42537762/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-26

#### 摘要

ETHNOPHARMACOLOGICAL RELEVANCE: Salvianolic acid A (SAA), a major bioactive constituent of Salvia miltiorrhiza, has attracted considerable attention because of its diverse pharmacological activities in metabolic and vascular disorders. Diabetic nephropathy (DN) is one of the most serious microvascular complications of diabetes and remains a leading cause of end-stage renal disease worldwide. Although increasing evidence has demonstrated the renoprotective effects of SAA, the precise molecular mechanisms underlying its therapeutic actions in DN remain incompletely understood.
AIM OF THE STUDY: This study aimed to systematically investigate the therapeutic mechanisms of SAA against DN by integrating network pharmacology, quantitative proteomics, molecular docking, and experimental validation.
MATERIALS AND METHODS: Potential therapeutic targets and signaling pathways of SAA in DN were identified through network pharmacology and quantitative proteomic analyses. A high-fat diet/streptozotocin-induced DN rat model was established to evaluate the renoprotective effects of SAA in vivo. Renal function, histopathological alterations, inflammatory responses, oxidative stress, mitochondrial homeostasis, autophagy-related proteins, and macrophage polarization were assessed. Molecular docking was performed to validate the interactions between SAA and key target proteins.
RESULTS: SAA significantly improved renal function and attenuated histopathological injury in DN rats. In addition, SAA reduced oxidative stress, suppressed inflammatory responses, restored mitochondrial homeostasis, and inhibited M1 macrophage polarization. Integrated network pharmacology and proteomic analyses identified the PI3K/Akt/mTOR signaling pathway as a critical target of SAA, which was further supported by molecular docking and experimental validation. Mechanistically, SAA inhibited PI3K/Akt/mTOR activation and restored autophagy-related signaling, thereby alleviating renal injury in DN.
CONCLUSION: SAA exerts renoprotective effects against DN through modulation of PI3K/Akt/mTOR-mediated autophagy and macrophage polarization, leading to attenuation of oxidative stress, inflammatory responses, mitochondrial dysfunction, and macrophage polarization. These findings provide mechanistic insights into the therapeutic potential of SAA and support its further development as a candidate treatment for DN.

### [C] Phytocannabinoid cannabidiol (CBD) in neurodegenerative diseases: From polypharmacology to drug development.

- PMID: 42790169
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: European journal of medicinal chemistry
- 作者: Zhang B, Nan J, Zhang Y, Zhang B, Ke C, Wang W, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec 15
- DOI: 10.1016/j.ejmech.2026.119360
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42790169/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-26

#### 摘要

Cannabidiol (CBD) is a non-intoxicating phytocannabinoid that has attracted interest as a multi-target neurotherapeutic candidate for neurodegenerative diseases. CBD is a lipophilic terpenophenolic chemotype whose phenolic redox chemistry, membrane partitioning, cytochrome P450-mediated metabolism, and formulation-dependent exposure are central to its biological activity and translational limitations. In addition, CBD engages a broad target network relevant to neurodegeneration. Major components include cannabinoid receptors, transient receptor potential channels, peroxisome proliferator-activated receptor-γ, adenosine and serotonin signaling systems, voltage-gated calcium channels, and redox-regulatory pathways. These mechanisms converge on neuroinflammation, excitotoxicity, mitochondrial dysfunction, impaired proteostasis, and synaptic injury. Preclinical studies across models of Alzheimer's disease, Parkinson's disease, multiple sclerosis, Huntington's disease, and amyotrophic lateral sclerosis consistently support CBD's anti-inflammatory, antioxidant, mitochondria-protective, and neuroprotective pharmacology. The magnitude of these effects depends on dose, treatment timing, model system, and route of administration. Clinical evidence remains preliminary and is mainly symptomatic, with signals in agitation, sleep disturbance, spasticity, quality of life, and neuropsychiatric symptoms rather than proven disease modification. Key barriers to development include low and variable oral bioavailability, incomplete brain exposure data, uncertain active metabolite contributions, limited target-engagement biomarkers, and insufficient exposure-response definition. Future development of CBD and optimized cannabinoid-derived analogues will require medicinal chemistry strategies to improve potency, selectivity, metabolic stability, CNS exposure, and formulation performance. Parallel biomarker-driven clinical trials are needed to define pharmacokinetic-pharmacodynamic relationships and evaluate disease-modifying potential.

- 作者: Xu Y, Namme J, Yang L, Xu Y, Biby S, Qian L, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec 15
- DOI: 10.1016/j.ejmech.2026.119319
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42735485/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-26

#### 摘要

In our continuing campaign to discover novel inhibitors of the nucleotide-binding oligomerization domain (NOD)-like receptor (NLR) protein 3 (NLRP3), an emerging drug target that tightly regulates the innate immune responses, a 1H-1,2,3-triazole-4-carboxamide scaffold was designed and optimized. Iterative optimization led to the identification of a lead inhibitor, 22, with an IC50 of 70 nM to suppress the release of interleukin (IL)-1β upon NLRP3 activation. Further in vivo studies supported its selective suppression of the NLRP3 inflammasome. Pharmacokinetic studies of 22 and PET imaging studies employing a F-18 tracer of 22 demonstrated 22 as a brain penetrant with reversible binding kinetics within the CNS. Importantly, treatment of AD patient-derived hiPSC glia cells including microglia and astrocytes and neuropathic pain mice with 22 led to suppression of NLRP3 expression and decreased pain and pain-like behavior, supporting the translational promise of this compound and analogs from this chemotype.

### [C] Silymarin attenuates neuroinflammation after subarachnoid hemorrhage by inducing M2 microglial polarization via activation of the JAK1/STAT6 signaling pathway.

- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42927074/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-26

#### 摘要

UNLABELLED: Neuroinflammation represents a key pathological process in early brain injury (EBI) following subarachnoid hemorrhage (SAH) and contributes significantly to poor outcomes. Silymarin, known for its anti-inflammatory and antioxidant activities, has been shown to exert neuroprotective effects. Nevertheless, the molecular mechanisms underlying these effects remain to be clarified. An in vivo endovascular perforation model and an in vitro hemoglobin-stimulated model were used to investigate the pharmacological effects of silymarin in SAH. Neurological function was assessed using SAH scores and neurobehavioral scores. Neuronal apoptosis was evaluated by TUNEL staining. Neuroinflammation was measured by ELISA or qPCR, and microglial polarization status was determined by assessing CD86 and CD206 expression via Western blotting (WB) or immunofluorescence (IF) staining. Ruxolitinib and AS1517499 were applied to block the JAK1/STAT6 pathway. Silymarin attenuated early brain injury (EBI) after SAH, as indicated by improved neurological scores, reduced neuronal apoptosis, and lower brain water content. In vivo, silymarin treatment was associated with JAK1/STAT6 activation, elevated M2 markers, and decreased pro‑inflammatory cytokines. In vitro, silymarin activated the JAK1/STAT6 pathway, promoted M2 microglial polarization, and suppressed inflammatory cytokine production. In addition, pharmacological blockade of JAK1/STAT6 reversed silymarin's protective effects, confirming a causal role of this pathway in the cellular context. Our in vitro data demonstrate that silymarin directly stimulates JAK1/STAT6 to drive M2 polarization and anti‑inflammatory responses. In vivo, we observed a correlation between pathway activation and neuroprotective benefits. Together, these findings provide a rationale for further exploring silymarin‑based therapies in SAH.
SUPPLEMENTARY INFORMATION: The online version contains supplementary material available at https://doi.org/10.1007/s10616-026-01073-w.

### [C] Protopanaxadiol-type ginsenosides ameliorate PTSD-like memory impairment and neuroinflammation in rats via suppression of TLR4/NF-κB signaling.

- DOI: 10.3892/etm.2026.13329
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42923872/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-26

#### 摘要

Post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD) is a serious psychiatric condition that develops after exposure to intense or traumatic stress. Ginsenosides, the primary bioactive compounds of ginseng, have been reported to enhance cognitive performance. Protopanaxadiol (PD) and protopanaxatriol (PT) are two major classes of triterpenoid saponins. In the present study, a PTSD-like rat model was established using the single prolonged stress (SPS) paradigm, and the effects of PD and PT on memory dysfunction were evaluated. After SPS exposure, male rats received daily intraperitoneal administration of PD (10 or 20 mg/kg) or PT (10 and 20 mg/kg) for 14 days. PD treatment significantly improved both spatial and cognitive memory compared with PT treatment. PD also suppressed the elevation of pro-inflammatory cytokines in the prefrontal cortex and hippocampus, and mitigated the reduction in brain-derived neurotrophic factor mRNA expression in the hippocampus. In addition, PD attenuated the upregulation of Toll-like receptor 4 (TLR4) and nuclear factor (NF)-κB protein expression in the hippocampus. By contrast, PT treatment did not produce meaningful changes in memory performance or inflammatory markers. These findings suggest that the beneficial effects of PD are associated with anti-inflammatory activity that may involve modulation of the TLR4/NF-κB signaling pathway.

#### 摘要

This study demonstrates that Galectin-3 (Gal3) exacerbates cerebral infarction progression by modulating the P2X7R/MerTK equilibrium in microglia via the ERK/BRD4/HIF1α signaling axis, with reactive oxygen species (ROS) serving as a potential intermediate mediator linking ERK activation to BRD4 upregulation based on prior literature evidence. Single-cell transcriptomic analysis revealed that ischemia drives microglia from a homeostatic state to an activated disease phenotype, and virtual Gal3 knockout predicted corresponding downregulation of BRD4. Using a rat model of cerebral infarction, we found that the Gal3 inhibitor TD-139 significantly reduced infarct volume, promoted M2 polarization of microglia, and improved neurological function in behavioral assays. Mechanistic investigations in BV2 microglia revealed that Gal3 activates ERK1/2, leading to ROS accumulation, BRD4 upregulation, and subsequent HIF1α-mediated P2X7R transcription, while suppressing KLF4 and MerTK. This cascade increased P2X7R expression and suppressed MerTK/KLF4 expression, generating a pro-inflammatory and pro-apoptotic microenvironment. Notably, pharmacological inhibition of Gal3 enhanced efferocytosis and reduced apoptosis, whereas P2X7R blockade exerted similar neuroprotective effects. These findings identify Gal3 as a key regulator of microglial polarization via the experimentally validated ERK/BRD4/HIF1α-P2X7R signaling cascade, where P2X7R activation concurrently triggers cGAS-STING-mediated pro-inflammatory/pyroptotic responses and suppresses the KLF4/MerTK efferocytosis axis. Targeting this regulatory network could provide therapeutic benefits in cerebral infarction by shifting microglia toward the neuroprotective M2 phenotype and facilitating inflammation resolution. Targeting this pathway could provide therapeutic benefits in cerebral infarction by shifting microglia toward the neuroprotective M2 phenotype and facilitating inflammation resolution. Data were analysed using one-way ANOVA followed by Tukey's post-hoc test for multiple comparisons; P < 0.05 was considered statistically significant.

### [C] Quality by design-assisted development of an intranasal Shikonin-loaded Mucoadhesive microemulsion for enhanced brain delivery: optimization, characterization, and Pharmacodynamic evaluation in a schizophrenia model.

- PMID: 42822353
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Bioorganic chemistry
- 作者: Inuganti PK, Gandla K, Repudi L, Majeed S, Muthukumarasamy R
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 05
- DOI: 10.1016/j.bioorg.2026.110594
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42822353/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-26

#### 摘要

The present study aimed to develop and optimize a shikonin-loaded mucoadhesive microemulsion for intranasal delivery with the objective of improving nasal residence and facilitating central drug delivery. Shikonin is a naturally occurring naphthoquinone derivative with reported anti-inflammatory and neuroprotective properties. A Quality by Design (QbD)-based strategy was employed to establish a robust formulation with defined critical quality attributes and improved nasal delivery characteristics. Solubility screening identified castor oil, Tween 80, and propylene glycol as the most suitable oil, surfactant, and co-surfactant, respectively. Pseudoternary phase diagram studies facilitated the selection of an optimal microemulsion region, and formulation optimization was performed using a Box-Behnken design by evaluating the effects of oil concentration, Smix concentration, and sonication time on critical quality attributes. The optimized formulation consisted of 10% oil, 30% Smix, and 10 min sonication time, producing nanosized droplets with a particle size below 100 nm, a polydispersity index below 0.30, a positive zeta potential of approximately +30 mV, and entrapment efficiency exceeding 85%. Chitosan was incorporated as a mucoadhesive polymer to increase formulation viscosity and interaction with the nasal mucosa, thereby promoting prolonged nasal residence and potentially facilitating drug absorption. The resulting formulation exhibited markedly increased viscosity and mucoadhesive strength, confirming improved nasal retention. Transmission electron microscopy confirmed the formation of uniformly distributed spherical nanodroplets. The optimized shikonin-loaded mucoadhesive microemulsion exhibited significantly improved in vitro drug release (87.3 ± 1.25% at 8 h) compared with the plain shikonin solution (69.2 ± 1.63%). Ex vivo permeation studies across sheep nasal mucosa demonstrated superior permeation from shikonin-loaded mucoadhesive microemulsion (91.2 ± 0.41%) relative to the drug solution (73.27 ± 0.14%), accompanied by higher steady-state flux and apparent permeability coefficient values. In ketamine-induced schizophrenia-like behavioral model rats, intranasal shikonin-loaded mucoadhesive microemulsion significantly reduced hyperlocomotion, improved motor coordination, and attenuated cataleptic behavior relative to the disease control group. Orally administered chlorpromazine was included as a pharmacological reference treatment; however, the findings were not interpreted as evidence of route-equivalent or therapeutic equivalence between the two treatments. Histopathological analyses of cortical and hippocampal tissues revealed preservation of neuronal architecture and attenuation of ketamine-associated tissue alterations. Collectively, these findings provide preliminary evidence supporting the potential of the optimized intranasal shikonin-loaded mucoadhesive microemulsion for central delivery and modulation of schizophrenia-like behavioral abnormalities. However, the formulation was evaluated at laboratory scale, and pilot-scale/large-scale manufacturing feasibility and scale-up validation remain to be established before further translational development.

### [C] Melatonin suppresses astrocyte reactivity and Mitigates cognitive deficits induced by a high-carbohydrate, high-fat diet in rats.

- PMID: 42932041
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Molecular biology reports
- 作者: Dorranipour D, Zangiabadi I, Peyvandi M, Naraki K, Basiri M
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 26
- DOI: 10.1007/s11033-026-12997-z
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42932041/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-27

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: High-carbohydrate and high-fat (HCHF) diets may impair cognitive function through neuroinflammation, oxidative stress, and astrocyte activation. This study investigated the effects of melatonin on memory impairment and astrocyte reactivity in rats with diet-induced metabolic syndrome.
METHODS AND RESULTS: Adult male Wistar rats were fed an HCHF diet for eight weeks, followed by daily oral melatonin treatment (5, 10, or 30 mg/kg) for four weeks while continuing the diet. Working memory was assessed using the Y-maze spontaneous alternation test. Hippocampal and hypothalamic tissues were evaluated for proinflammatory cytokines, oxidative stress markers, and glial fibrillary acidic protein (GFAP) immunoreactivity. HCHF feeding caused weight gain, dyslipidemia, glucose intolerance, impaired working memory, increased neuroinflammation and oxidative damage, and astrogliosis in both brain regions. Melatonin did not improve glucose tolerance or lipid profiles at any dose. However, working memory performance in melatonin-treated rats was comparable to that of controls at all doses. Melatonin also reduced GFAP-positive astrocytes, proinflammatory cytokine levels, and lipid peroxidation, while increasing superoxide dismutase activity. These effects were mainly dose-dependent in the hippocampus, whereas memory improvement showed no clear dose-dependent pattern.
CONCLUSIONS: Melatonin improved working memory and reduced astrocyte reactivity, neuroinflammation, and oxidative stress in rats fed an HCHF diet, without improving systemic metabolic abnormalities. These findings suggest that melatonin may protect against diet-induced brain injury through mechanisms related to astrocyte activation, inflammation, and oxidative stress.

### [C] Octyl gallate attenuates atherosclerosis by modulating PPARγ-associated cholesterol homeostasis and enhancing NRF2-NQO1 antioxidant defence.

- PMID: 42929773
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Food & function
- 作者: Hua J, Yue X, Wu C, Wang J
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 26
- DOI: 10.1039/d6fo03393h
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42929773/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-27

#### 摘要

Atherosclerosis (AS) is a vascular disease driven by dysregulated lipid metabolism, oxidative stress, and chronic inflammation. This study investigated the anti-atherosclerotic potential of octyl gallate (OG), a gallic acid derivative with anti-inflammatory and metabolic regulatory properties. In vitro, OG (20-50 μM) significantly attenuated oxidised low-density lipoprotein (ox-LDL)-induced lipid accumulation in THP-1-derived macrophages. This effect was accompanied by increased expression of the cholesterol efflux-related genes ABCA1 and ABCG1, whereas OG had only limited effects on CD36 and SRA expression. In vivo, oral administration of OG (25 or 50 mg kg-1) for 3 months reduced atherosclerotic lesion formation in the aortic arch, whole aorta and aortic root of high-fat diet-fed Apoe-/- mice. OG also improved serum lipid profiles by reducing low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C) and total cholesterol levels, increasing high-density lipoprotein cholesterol (HDL-C) levels. In addition, OG decreased IL-1β and TNF-α expression in the aortic root. Network pharmacology, molecular docking and molecular dynamics simulations identified PPARγ as a potential molecular target of OG. Further experiments demonstrated that OG enhanced the expression of PPARγ, LXRα, ABCA1, and ABCG1 in ox-LDL-treated macrophages. Moreover, OG suppressed inflammatory responses and reactive oxygen species (ROS) accumulation while activating the NRF2-NQO11 antioxidant system and promoting NRF2 nuclear translocation. Collectively, these findings suggest that OG may exert anti-atherosclerotic effects by coordinating macrophage lipid metabolism, redox homeostasis, and inflammatory responses through the PPARγ-LXRα-ABCA1/ABCG1 axis and the NRF2-NQO1 antioxidant pathway, respectively. These results provide experimental evidence supporting the potential of OG as a candidate compound for the prevention and treatment of AS.

### [C] Transcutaneous auricular vagus nerve stimulation attenuates post-stroke dysphagia by modulating microglial polarization via the endothelial VWF/PI3K-Akt axis.

- PMID: 42927825

- 期刊: Brain research
- 作者: Yu F, Feng Y, Miao Y, Xu G
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 25
- DOI: 10.1016/j.brainres.2026.150605
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42927825/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-27

#### 摘要

Post-stroke dysphagia is a common complication with limited treatment options. Transcutaneous auricular vagus nerve stimulation (ta-VNS) has shown neuroprotective effects. This study investigated whether ta-VNS improves swallowing function and alleviates neuroinflammation by modulating microglial M1/M2 polarization, examining the role of von Willebrand factor (VWF) in endothelial-microglial crosstalk. A rat model of transient bilateral middle cerebral artery occlusion (MCAO) was established. Rats received ta-VNS for 3 weeks, and swallowing function, neurological deficits, and inflammatory markers were assessed. Microglial M1 (CD16) and M2 (CD206) polarization in the white matter was examined by immunofluorescence. RNA-seq was used to identify candidate genes. In vitro, an oxygen-glucose deprivation/reoxygenation (OGD/R) model was applied to hCMEC/D3 endothelial cells co-cultured with HMC3 microglia, and VWF was knocked down using shRNA to evaluate its effect on microglial polarization and inflammation. ta-VNS significantly improved body weight, food/water intake, and swallowing function (reduced latency and increased swallowing frequency) in transient middle cerebral artery occlusion rats. ta-VNS reduced levels of TNF-α, IL-6, IL-1β, and IL-10, attenuated microglial activation, suppressed M1 (Iba-1+CD16+) microglia, and enhanced M2 (Iba-1+CD206+) polarization in rat whiter matter. RNA-seq revealed downregulation of VWF after ta-VNS, with enrichment in the PI3K-Akt pathway. In vitro, OGD/R upregulated VWF, ICAM-1, VCAM-1, endothelial apoptosis, and permeability. Knockdown of VWF in endothelial cells shifted co-cultured microglia from M1 toward M2 phenotype and reduced TNF-α, IL-6, and IL-1β while increasing IL-10. ta-VNS can improve post-stroke dysphagia and reduce neuroinflammation by promoting M2 microglial polarization. Endothelial VWF may be a key downstream mediator of this process.

### [C] Persistent microbial-derived material accumulates after brain injury and associates with chronic neuroinflammation.

- PMID: 42927796
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Brain, behavior, and immunity
- 作者: Yang H, Shyong O, Wu L, Marcet E, Dillon B, Dillon J, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 25
- DOI: 10.1016/j.bbi.2026.107082
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42927796/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-27

#### 摘要

OBJECTIVE: Traumatic brain injury (TBI) is associated with chronic neurodegenerative sequelae, including chronic traumatic encephalopathy (CTE), but mechanisms sustaining long-term neuroinflammation in the injured brain remain unclear. Persistent, poorly cleared biological material has been proposed as a driver of chronic inflammation. This study tested whether microbial-derived material is present in CTE and experimental brain injury, and whether reducing such material is associated with improved outcomes.
METHODS: Post-mortem brain tissue from individuals with CTE and controls was analyzed for the microbial polysaccharide poly-N-acetyl-glucosamine (PNAG) and phosphorylated tau (pTau). Mice were subjected to single and repetitive closed head injury (CHI), with assessment of brain PNAG and tau accumulation, behavior, and neuropathology. Purified PNAG was administered intracerebrally to evaluate biological effects. Passive antibody treatment and active vaccination targeting PNAG were used to determine whether reducing brain PNAG altered outcomes after CHI.
RESULTS: PNAG accumulated in human CTE brains in a disease stage-dependent manner and colocalized with pTau. In mice, PNAG rapidly accumulated across multiple brain regions after CHI and remained elevated at two weeks. Intracerebral administration of purified PNAG induced physiological and behavioral deficits that differed by sex, whereas a lower dose produced no detectable behavioral deficits. Passive anti-PNAG antibody treatment reduced brain PNAG with limited functional benefit, whereas active vaccination in adolescence and early adulthood reduced brain PNAG accumulation and IBA1 immunoreactivity, was associated with lower pTau accumulation, and improved long-term motor outcomes one year after repetitive CHI.
CONCLUSIONS: Persistent microbial-derived material is detectable in human CTE and experimental TBI. Reduction of PNAG-associated microbial-derived material is associated with attenuation of chronic neuroinflammation, pathological tau accumulation, and improved long-term neurological outcomes, supporting a model in which persistent microbial-derived material may contribute to chronic post-traumatic neuroinflammation.

### [C] Peripheral immune tolerance as a driver of tumor immune escape and therapy resistance.

- PMID: 42927598
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Clinical & translational oncology : official publication of the Federation of Spanish Oncology Societies and of the National Cancer Institute of Mexico
- 作者: Vaishnave S, Jayaraj A, Selvaraj J, Ramachandiran S, Natarajan M, Gunasekaran B, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 25
- DOI: 10.1007/s12094-026-04590-4
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42927598/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-27

#### 摘要

Peripheral immune tolerance, essential for preventing autoimmunity, is increasingly co-opted by tumors to drive progression, metastatic dissemination, and immunotherapy resistance. Whereas central tolerance eliminates autoreactive clones, peripheral tolerance remains dynamically regulated by antigen persistence, cytokine gradients, metabolic cues, and stromal architecture, a plasticity that tumors exploit to build a multilayered immunosuppressive ecosystem. This narrative review synthesizes multi-omic, spatial, cellular, and molecular evidence, emphasizing 2021 to 2025 publications alongside foundational studies, to propose tumor-induced peripheral tolerance (TIPT) as a unifying framework for immune escape and treatment failure. TIPT emerges from coordinated suppression by tumor-resident regulatory T cells, myeloid-derived suppressor cells, regulatory B cells, M2-like macrophages, tolerogenic dendritic cells, and exhausted CD8? T cells, sustained by suppressive cytokine networks, metabolic deprivation, epigenetic fixation of dysfunctional states, and stromal-vascular exclusion barriers. Spatially organized tolerance niches are associated with immune evasion, metastatic seeding, and resistance to checkpoint blockade. Strategies targeting suppressive cell populations, metabolic pathways, chromatin regulators, and stromal barriers are in clinical development, but clinical benefit has been established for only a limited subset, and anti-TIGIT antibodies failed to meet primary endpoints in phase 3 lung cancer trials. Machine-learning models show promise for predicting tolerance states and guiding combination design, but prospective clinical validation is still needed. TIPT may represent an important, potentially actionable determinant of tumor evolution and immunotherapy response, and durable cancer control is likely to require combined strategies that dismantle this tolerance ecosystem.

### [C] Design, synthesis, and biological evaluation of novel ebselen-chalcone hybrids as multifunctional neuroprotective agents for ischemic stroke.

- PMID: 42815280
- Domain: Out-of-scope

- 转化相关性: Low

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Bioorganic chemistry
- 作者: Chen YL, Yan FT, Luo D, Ma LF, Hong WY, Zhan ZJ
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 05
- DOI: 10.1016/j.bioorg.2026.110582
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42815280/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-27

#### 摘要

Ischemic stroke is a multifactorial neurological disorder characterized by oxidative stress, neuroinflammation, and mitochondrial dysfunction. Currently, effective neuroprotective agents for treating ischemic stroke remain limited. To integrate the glutathione peroxidase (GPx)-mimicking activity of ebselen with the antioxidant and anti-inflammatory properties of chalcones, a total of 67 ebselen-chalcone hybrids were rationally designed and synthesized. Biological evaluation identified B21 as an initial lead compound with improved neuroprotective activity and reduced cytotoxicity compared to ebselen, as reflected by an enhanced selectivity index (SI > 6.37 vs 2.43). Subsequent structural optimization led to the identification of C13, which exhibited superior neuroprotective and anti-inflammatory activities with a markedly improved safety (SI > 50.31). Furthermore, in vivo bioassay demonstrated that C13 showed good tolerability and exerted significant neuroprotective effects in a middle cerebral artery occlusion (MCAO) model, comparable to the positive control edaravone. Mechanistic studies revealed that C13 effectively reduced intracellular reactive oxygen species accumulation and preserved mitochondrial membrane potential, primarily through activation of the Nrf2/HO-1 signaling pathway. Overall, this study highlights the effectiveness of an ebselen-based molecular hybridization strategy and identifies C13 as a promising lead compound for ischemic stroke therapy.

### [C] Research progress in ocular manifestations of hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis: Clinical features, mechanisms and therapeutic perspectives (Review).

- PMID: 42923866
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Biomedical reports
- 作者: Wu W, Zhou Y, Wu R, Yang M, Wang Y, Wang K
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.3892/br.2026.2208
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42923866/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-27

#### 摘要

Hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis (HLH) is a cytokine storm-driven immune dysregulation syndrome in which ocular involvement remains critically underreported. Although isolated case reports have documented ocular findings since 1968, no prior work has systematically mapped the full phenotypic spectrum across all ocular structures, to the best of our knowledge. This gap spans both ophthalmology and hematology, where such manifestations are routinely misattributed to primary infection, autoimmunity or neoplasia. Addressing this lack of information, the present scoping review consolidates 40 peer-reviewed sources via a structured PubMed scoping search, providing the first dedicated synthesis of HLH ocular disease, to the best of our knowledge. Among the published case reports, uveitis, particularly intermediate uveitis or panuveitis, was frequently reported, and retinal pathology encompassed hemorrhage, vascular occlusive changes, serous detachment and distinct acute macular neuroretinopathy-like outer retinal atrophy. Optic nerve involvement may present as disc edema or optic neuritis, occasionally antedating systemic diagnosis, and orbital inflammation frequently mimics cellulitis. Multimodal imaging, including optical coherence tomography, optical coherence tomography angiography and fluorescein fundus angiography, delineates microstructural and microvascular alterations, contextualizing infectious etiologies, such as cytomegalovirus, human herpesvirus 8 and fungal retinitis, alongside primary HLH phenotypes. Consistent with the heterogeneous nature of the current evidence, the present scoping review maps these phenotypes without differentiating between direct HLH-mediated injury and sequelae related to immunosuppression or therapy. Despite the absence of standardized classifications, these findings underscore that ocular assessment may be informative for the early recognition and interdisciplinary management of atypical HLH presentations.

- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42858589/

#### 摘要

Pseudorabies virus (PRV), the causative agent of Aujeszky's disease in pigs, threatens swine production and can infect other mammals, with rare zoonotic cases reported. Effective antiviral agents against PRV remain lacking. Here, we constructed and purified a recombinant fusion protein combining porcine Interleukin-2 (IL-2), interferon α (IFN-α), and Thymosin α1 (Tα1). The fusion protein showed no cytotoxicity or induction of apoptosis in PK-15 cells. In vitro, it significantly enhanced peripheral blood mononuclear cell (PBMC) metabolic activity and upregulated expression of key interferon-stimulated genes (ISGs) involved in antiviral defense, including IFIT1, ISG15, MX1, and OAS, which are critical for antiviral activity. Analysis of the viral life cycle indicated that Interleukin-2-Interferon-α-Thymosin α1 (IL-2-IFN-α-Tα1) inhibited PRV replication without affecting viral attachment, entry, assembly, or egress. Treatment with the fusion protein conferred marked therapeutic efficacy against PRV infection, including improved survival, reduced viral loads in the brain and spleen, and alleviated inflammatory pathology. These results demonstrate that IL-2-IFN-α-Tα1 fusion protein has potential as a novel immunomodulatory agent for controlling PRV infection in swine, offering a promising strategy for antiviral intervention in veterinary medicine.

#### 摘要

Stereotactic radiosurgery emerged as a neurosurgical discipline in order to utilize energy for the manipulation of brain or nerve tissue, with the goal of minimal access and safe and effective care of a spectrum of neurosurgical disorders. Perhaps no other branch of neurosurgery has been so disruptive across the entire discipline of brain tumor care, treatment of vascular disorders, and management of functional problems. Radiosurgery is mainstream, supported by thousands of peer-reviewed outcomes reports. This article reviews current practice with a focus on challenges, emerging trends, and areas of investigation.

- DOI: 10.3171/CASE26220
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42933003/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-30

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Sacral spinal dural arteriovenous fistulas (sDAVFs) are rare and easily missed on imaging, especially when supplied by internal iliac branches. Regional vascular anatomy may complicate endovascular treatment approaches.
OBSERVATIONS: A 71-year-old man with symptoms suggestive of spinal myelopathy underwent spinal MRI, which revealed multisegment T2 cord hyperintensity from T7 to L1 without serpiginous flow voids; spinal MR angiography (MRA) findings were negative for sDAVF. On spinal digital subtraction angiography (DSA), selective internal iliac injection identified a right S1 sDAVF originating from a lateral sacral artery branch. Transarterial Onyx embolization formed a plug proximal to the fistula, resulting in failed obliteration. Subsequent microsurgical disconnection via S1 laminectomy appeared successful on intraoperative ICG angiography, but postoperative angiography revealed residual shunting. Reexploration with L5 laminectomy allowed definitive microsurgical ligation, resulting in lasting cure at the 1-year follow-up.
LESSONS: Negative spinal MRI/MRA findings do not rule out sacral sDAVF; complete spinal DSA, including internal iliac injection, is critical if sacral sDAVF is suspected. Sacral angioarchitecture may hinder durable embolization, warranting consideration of upfront microsurgical ligation. With ligation, intraoperative ICG angiography may miss residual flow; immediate postoperative angiography should be best practice. With microsurgical ligation, broad exposures may be ideal for identifying all arterialized veins. https://thejns.org/doi/10.3171/CASE26220.
