- 作者: Bhandari V, Garg D, Agarwal A, Anand A, Srivastava AK
- 发表日期: 2026 Sep
- DOI: 10.7759/cureus.117001
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42948773/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-30

#### 摘要

Dural arteriovenous fistulas (dAVFs) are rare intracranial vascular abnormalities that may present with varied and sometimes atypical clinical features. While proptosis is commonly associated with anterior cranial or cavernous sinus fistulas, it is unusual in posterior fossa dAVFs. We present the case of a 41-year-old man with progressive redness, swelling, and protrusion of the left eye over six months, without associated pain, diplopia, headache, or cranial nerve deficits. Ocular examination revealed preserved visual acuity and extraocular movements, with normal fundus findings. An initial diagnosis of a cavernous sinus fistula was considered. However, brain MRI with time-of-flight sequences revealed a left temporal dAVF with feeders from the external carotid artery and drainage into the vein of Labbé and transverse sinus, with a possible connection to the superior division of the middle cerebral artery. On clinical re-examination, a bruit was noted in the left occipitotemporal region. Doppler and transcranial Doppler studies confirmed mixed arterial and venous flow. The patient was referred for digital subtraction angiography and embolization. The symptoms of dAVFs vary depending on the shunt characteristics, location, and venous drainage pattern. Ocular signs are uncommon in noncavernous dAVFs. In this case, venous reflux through the vein of Labbé may explain the isolated proptosis. Management is guided by angioarchitecture and associated risk features, with high-risk fistulas requiring timely intervention. This case highlights the need to consider cranial dural AVFs in the differential diagnosis of isolated proptosis, as timely diagnosis and treatment are crucial.

### [C] Nodose ganglion vagal afferents promote electroacupuncture efficacy in the gut-brain axis via proteasome restoration and CXCL10 suppression.

- PMID: 42955854
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Neural regeneration research
- 作者: Li J, Yang J, Wang J, Xie G, Zheng Z, Huang Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 29
- DOI: 10.4103/NRR.NRRONLINE-D-26-00868
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42955854/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-30

#### 摘要

Electroacupuncture is a potent neuromodulatory strategy for mitigating intestinal pathologies. Nevertheless, the role of primary visceral sensory apparatuses in transducing somatosensory acupuncture signals remains unclear. Here, we sought to delineate the neuroanatomical necessity and cell type-specific molecular reprogramming within the nodose ganglion, the primary sensory relay of vagal afferents, underlying electroacupuncture-mediated intestinal homeostasis. Retrograde tracing confirmed a direct neuroanatomical conduit between the distal colon and nodose ganglion sensory neurons. Under pathological conditions, the nodose ganglion dynamically sensed colonic inflammation, manifesting as profound transcriptomic shifts. Electroacupuncture at Zusanli (ST36) significantly attenuated UC severity, suppressed systemic inflammation, and restored both physical (zonula occludens-1 and claudin-1) and chemical (Muc2 and Tff3) mucosal barriers. Crucially, targeted ablation of nodose ganglion vagal afferents completely abolished these macroscopic and molecular protective effects, along with the blockade of electroacupuncture-evoked cervical vagal discharge and colonic motility reflexes. Multomic deconvolution revealed that colitis triggered severe proteasome dysregulation and aberrant Cxcl10-mediated satellite glia/macrophage-to-neuron crosstalk within the nodose ganglion. Remarkably, electroacupuncture precisely reversed this pathological reprogramming by upregulating the expression of core proteasome-associated hub genes (Psma2, Psmd14, and Eif2s1) and potently quenching Cxcl10-induced neuroimmune hypersensitivity. These findings suggest that vagal sensory afferents within the nodose ganglion function as indispensable anatomical bottlenecks and functional responders for electroacupuncture-mediated colitis remission. By expanding the classic "acupoint-organ-center" paradigm, these findings demonstrate that disease-state sensory ganglia actively gate neuromodulatory therapy, providing a high-resolution molecular foundation for the clinical translation of electroacupuncture in treating intestinal disease.

### [C] Vitamin supplementation and Alzheimer's disease: what the evidence shows.

- PMID: 42955064
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Panminerva medica
- 作者: Gordon L, Buyuksandalyaci Tunc E, Vaikutis L, Grossberg GT
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 29
- DOI: 10.23736/S0031-0808.26.05448-0
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42955064/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-30

#### 摘要

Alzheimer's disease (AD) is the most common cause of major neurocognitive disorder worldwide, with growing prevalence and limited disease-modifying treatment options. Vitamin supplementation has attracted considerable interest as a potentially modifiable strategy in AD because several vitamins are involved in biological pathways implicated in AD pathogenesis, including oxidative stress, neuroinflammation, and homocysteine metabolism. However, the clinical relevance of these mechanistic associations remains uncertain, and findings from interventional studies have been inconsistent. This narrative review critically evaluates the current evidence regarding vitamin supplementation across the AD spectrum, including associations between vitamin deficiency and AD risk, the potential role of supplementation in AD prevention, and cognitive or functional outcomes among individuals with mild cognitive impairment or established AD. Among the vitamins reviewed, folic acid and vitamin B12 demonstrate the most consistent, although still limited, signal for potential cognitive benefit, particularly when used in combination although whether this benefit is concentrated among individuals with elevated homocysteine levels remains uncertain. Vitamin D deficiency has been relatively consistently associated with increased AD risk in observational studies, supporting consideration of vitamin D assessment and correction of documented deficiency as part of routine clinical care in older adults. Evidence for vitamin E suggests possible attenuation of functional decline at higher doses but does not support routine use for cognitive benefit or AD prevention. For vitamins A, C, thiamine, and niacin, clinical evidence remains sparse, indirect, and insufficient to support supplementation beyond correction of documented deficiencies. Overall, biological plausibility has consistently outpaced demonstrated clinical efficacy in vitamin literature. Vitamin supplementation should not be considered a disease-modifying intervention for AD; however, vitamin assessment, including baseline serum vitamin levels where clinically indicated, and supplementation should be individualized, with correction of documented deficiencies remaining appropriate for general health, particularly in older adults with poor intake, malabsorption risk, or medication-related deficiencies. Ensuring adequate B vitamin intake and correcting vitamin D deficiency when documented may be incorporated into a broader brain-health strategy alongside pharmacologic treatments and lifestyle interventions. Future research should prioritize adequately powered clinical trials with standardized outcomes, longer follow-up periods, and stratification by baseline nutritional status.

- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42954922/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-30

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Intracerebral haemorrhage (ICH) is a severe stroke subtype with high morbidity and mortality, where inflammation plays a crucial role. Recent studies suggest that the RANKL/RANK pathway is involved in stroke pathophysiology. This study investigated the effects of recombinant RANKL on microglial polarization after ICH.
METHODS: In vitro, BV2 cells were stimulated with erythrocyte lysis (ELS) to assess cell viability, migration, and microglial phenotype markers. Stattic was used to block STAT3 pathway, and microglial markers were measured by RT-qPCR. In vivo, ICH rats were treated with RANKL or OPG. Neurological deficits and brain injury were evaluated. Immunohistochemistry analysed microglial activation and M1/M2 marker expression.
RESULTS: Recombinant RANKL enhanced BV2 cell migration induced by ELS and shifted microglial polarization towards M2, with reduced M1 marker expression. The increase in M2 markers was inhibited by Stattic, which blocks STAT3 signalling. In vivo, RANKL-treated rats showed reduced brain edema and improved neurobehavioral outcomes after ICH. RANKL treatment promoted M2 polarization of microglia/macrophages and inhibited M1 polarization following ICH.
CONCLUSIONS: RANKL plays a key role in ICH by promoting microglia M2 polarization and inhibiting M1 polarization.

#### 摘要

PURPOSE: Insular infraorbital neurovascular pedicle labial salivary gland transplantation (IINPLSGT) is an innovative procedure for the treatment of severe dry eye disease (DED). We aim to evaluate changes in clinical parameters, tear osmolarity, inflammatory cytokine levels, and proteomic profiles following IINPLSGT for severe dry eye patients.
METHODS: A single-center, prospective exploratory study was conducted in 10 patients diagnosed with severe DED who underwent IINPLSGT surgery. The Ocular Surface Disease Index (OSDI), tear break-up time (TBUT), Schirmer I test (SIt), corneal fluorescein staining (CFS), ocular manifestations and tear osmolarity were assessed before and after treatment. Tear inflammatory cytokines were analyzed using ELISA; Tear proteomes were characterized using liquid chromatography mass spectrometry.
RESULTS: Six months after surgery, improvements were observed in the patients' OSDI, TBUT, SIt, CFS, symblepharon and corneal opacification (p < .05). Tear osmolarity and levels of Interleukin (IL)-1β, IL-6, and tumor necrosis factor-α were significantly reduced (p < .05). A total of 111 differentially expressed proteins (DEPs) were identified between preoperative and postoperative samples. Protein dysregulation was associated with inflammatory response. Upregulated proteins were related to mitochondrial energy metabolism and cellular repair. Enrichment analysis revealed that these DEPs were involved in extracellular vesicle, actin-myosin filament sliding and antigen binding. Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes pathway analysis highlighted key roles of the PI3K/AKT, MAPK signaling pathways, cytokine-cytokine receptor interaction and DNA replication.
CONCLUSIONS: IINPLSGT was associated with improvements in symptoms and clinical signs of severe DED, accompanied by reductions in tear osmolarity and inflammatory cytokine levels during follow-up. Postoperative tear proteomic analysis further suggests that the protein profiles of secretions from saliva may partially overlap with natural tear protein profiles. These exploratory findings highlight the therapeutic potential of IINPLSGT, but warrant confirmation in larger, controlled studies.
TRIAL REGISTRATION NUMBER: ChiCTR2200056015.

### [C] Vitis vinifera leaf extract improves quality-of-life and significantly decreases IL-6 levels in patients with chronic venous disease: a prospective, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study.

- 作者: Psachoulia F, Karathanos C, Antonopoulos C, Kardoulas D, Sfyroeras G, Chania C, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 29
- DOI: 10.23736/S0392-9590.26.05549-5
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42954789/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-30

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Phlebotonic medication is an established conservative management approach for Chronic Venous Disease (CVD). This study evaluates whether a new formulation of red vine leaf-containing supplement relieves symptoms, improves quality-of-life and affects oxidative stress and inflammation in C2-C3 CVD patients.
METHODS: This prospective, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study enrolled 58 participants, allocated 1:1 to self-administer the Vitis vinifera supplement (N.=30) or placebo (N.=28), orally for 8 weeks. Assessments were conducted at baseline and post-intervention. Primary endpoints included changes in clinical symptomatology (Visual Analogue Scale/VAS), leg oedema and calf veins diameter. Secondary outcomes assessed quality-of-life (CIVIQ-20), biomarkers of oxidative stress (malondialdehyde/MDA, protein carbonyls/PCs), inflammation (C-Reactive Protein/CRP, Interleukin-6/IL-6) and endothelial function (Cyclic Guanosine Monophosphate/cGMP) and adverse events incidence. Data analysis was conducted using SPSS and GraphPad Prism.
RESULTS: Mean age was 55.4±11.1 years in the intervention group and 56.2±14.4 in the placebo (P=0.82). Post-treatment, numbness/tingling VAS score significantly reduced in the intervention group compared to placebo (3±3.4 versus 1.33±2.7 points; P=0.040). CIVIQ-20 score reduction was also significantly greater (15.9±14 versus 5.9±15.1; P=0.011). At baseline, there were no significant differences between the V. vinifera supplement and placebo groups in mean ankle or calf circumference. After 8 weeks, both parameters remained comparable between groups. IL-6 levels were significantly reduced in the intervention group to 80.82±8.27pg/mL (baseline 114.7±8.87 pg/mL; P=0.015), whereas the placebo showed a non-significant marginal increase to 103.7±13.55 pg/mL (baseline 89.91±9.2 pg/mL; P=0.41).
CONCLUSIONS: We demonstrate that the V. vinifera-derived supplement reduces numbness/tingling and improves quality-of-life in C2-C3 CVD patients, potentially through inflammatory biomarkers modulation.

### [C] Fibroproliferative Remodeling of Immune-Vascular Co-Culture Spheroid Models in Response to Atherogenic Stimulation.

- PMID: 42954076
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 期刊: Biotechnology and bioengineering
- 作者: Naiyeju I, Lehoux S, Tabrizian M
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 29
- DOI: 10.1002/bit.70435
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42954076/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-30

#### 摘要

Cell-based models are highly valuable platforms for preclinical studies of atherosclerosis, offering controllable environments to investigate disease mechanisms. Three-dimensional (3D) spheroids, a class of cell-based models, enable the study of atherosclerotic disease phenomena under physiologically relevant yet controlled conditions. In this study, we examined the lipid-induced changes in a co-culture spheroid model comprising THP-1-derived macrophages and primary human coronary artery smooth muscle cells (HCASMCs) following treatment with 20 or 40 µg/mL oxidized LDL (ox-LDL), a known atherogenic agent. Over a 10-day period, we assessed changes in spheroid morphology (size and compactness), cell viability, metabolic activity, intracellular composition, gene expression, pro-inflammatory cytokine release and extracellular matrix deposition, relative to the untreated control. Lipid exposure increased the abundance of macrophages in ox-LDL-treated spheroids (LS20-3 h: 35.5 ± 21.9%; LS40-3 h: 38.3 ± 8.7%) compared with controls (21.8 ± 0.7%) as early as day 3. Upregulation of fibronectin (LS20-3 h: ~1.8-fold; LS40-3 h: ~2.1-fold), CD36 (~1.8-fold), and ABCA-1 (~1.9-fold) was also observed. Moreover, spheroids exposed to 40 µg/mL ox-LDL exhibited reduced viability and compactness compared with those treated with 20 µg/mL by day 10. While lipid-treated spheroids showed non-statistically significantly higher levels of pro-inflammatory IL-1β cytokine expression at day 0 which declined (> 85% decrease) by day 3, all spheroid groups showed comparable global fibronectin ECM secretion indices. Collectively, these findings reveal lipid-induced proliferative changes to spheroid composition & functionality and show their potential as biomimetic models for the study of atherosclerotic disease phenomena.

### [C] Mesenchymal stem cell-derived spheroids and microtissues for biofabrication: advances in bioassembly and bioprinting strategies.

- PMID: 42952271
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Biofabrication
- 作者: Contreras-Pérez DA, Hoffmann A, Egger D
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 29
- DOI: 10.1088/1758-5090/aeb1fa
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42952271/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-30

#### 摘要

Biofabrication integrates biological and engineering principles to generate functional tissues for regenerative medicine, drug screening, and disease modeling. Among the diverse cellular sources available, mesenchymal stem cells (MSCs) have emerged as a critical component due to their multipotency, immunomodulatory capacity, and scalability. This review focuses on MSC-derived spheroids and microtissues as building blocks for biofabrication, organizing recent advances in bioassembly and bioprinting strategies around the axis of undifferentiated versus pre-differentiated microtissues and how the timing of differentiation shapes construct functionality. Key technological innovations, including dynamic culture systems, scaffold-free assembly, and high-resolution bioprinting (e.g. droplet-based), have enabled enhanced control over microtissue maturation and structural fidelity. The role of the cellular microenvironment in determining MSC fate is examined, emphasizing how biochemical cues, mechanical properties, and oxygen gradients can be engineered during microtissue formation. Cellular heterogeneity is addressed as a path-dependent variable continuously reshaped by donor-specific factors (age, sex, health status) and process-imposed cues (isolation methods, expansion conditions, and mechanical stress) throughout thein vitrolife cycle, and concrete mitigation strategies are discussed, including prospective cell sorting and a proposed functional potency panel (extracellular matrix deposition, fusion competence, and secretome profiling), alongside recent advancements in automated and machine learning-based biofabrication platforms. Vascularization and immunological integration are examined as central translation bottlenecks, covering endothelial co-culture, prevascularized spheroids, sacrificial bioprinting, and MSC-driven modulation of the host immune response. By integrating the fabrication route, heterogeneity as a manufacturing process variable, and translational/regulatory bottlenecks into a single framework, this review highlights recent advances, discusses current limitations, and outlines future directions for clinical translation of MSC-based biofabrication.

### [C] Inflammation in coronary syndromes in clinical practice: a clinical consensus statement of the Association for Acute Cardiovascular Care of the European Society of Cardiology, the European Society of Cardiology Working Group on Myocardial and Pericardial Diseases, the European Society of Cardiology Council on Basic Cardiovascular Science, and the European Society of Cardiology Council for Cardiology Practice.

- PMID: 42536391
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: European heart journal. Acute cardiovascular care
- 作者: Roubille F, Kharlamov JA, Lenz M, Madonna R, Delmas C, Schaubroeck H, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 29
- DOI: 10.1093/ehjacc/zuag086
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42536391/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-30

#### 摘要

Inflammation is a key driver of coronary syndromes across the continuum from chronic coronary syndromes to acute coronary syndromes. It contributes to atherogenesis, plaque destabilization, and ischaemic events and influences myocardial injury, repair, and remodelling. Inflammatory activity is further amplified by metabolic and autoimmune comorbidities such as diabetes, obesity, and connective tissue diseases, with obesity representing a major driver of chronic low-grade inflammation through adipose tissue dysfunction and cytokine activation, while psychosocial stress and environmental exposures including air pollution serve as additional triggers. Genetic variations, including human leucocyte antigen genotypes, modulate individual susceptibility to vascular inflammation and myocardial injury. This expert consensus from the European Society of Cardiology's Association for Acute CardioVascular Care summarizes the current understanding of inflammation across the full spectrum of coronary syndromes, including underlying mechanisms, relevant biomarkers, and imaging approaches that characterize vascular inflammation, and integrates insights from experimental and clinical studies. Importantly, this document provides explicit consensus-based guidance on when, how, and in whom inflammation should be assessed and treated in routine clinical practice. While the causal association between inflammation and atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease is well established, pharmacological modification of inflammation has produced conflicting evidence. Some agents, such as low-dose colchicine, have demonstrated modest reductions in cardiovascular events, although results across trials have been inconsistent. Other anti-inflammatory therapies have not shown clinical benefit, whereas selective cytokine inhibition with canakinumab has reduced cardiovascular risk at the cost of increased infection rates. The present manuscript also focuses on the evaluation of inflammatory activity in cardiovascular patients in routine clinical practice. It further discusses criteria for patient selection and clinical decision-making regarding the introduction of anti-inflammatory therapy in individuals with established atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease.

- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 30
- DOI: 10.1126/sciadv.ady5085
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42962014/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

Formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded (FFPE) tissue-compatible spatial transcriptomics (ST) is transforming biology and histopathology, but achieving high sensitivity, genome-wide coverage, single-cell resolution, and researcher accessibility remains challenging. To address these challenges, we here present a dendrimeric DNA coordinate barcoding design for FFPE tissue-adapted spatial RNA sequencing (Decoder-FFPE-seq) by integrating RNA-to-DNA probe conversion with blotting-assisted barcoding. Decoder-FFPE-seq provides single-cell resolution with a >2.6-fold sensitivity increase in whole-transcriptome mouse brain and archived human sample profiling over existing platforms, resolving potential cellular identity of low-abundance genes, finer tissue architecture, and capturing an epithelial-mesenchymal transition-associated signature for prognostic value. With expanded probe libraries, Decoder-FFPE-seq captures long noncoding and viral RNAs, revealing their spatial coregulatory networks with messenger RNAs in oral squamous cell carcinomas (OSCCs). In an OSCC immunotherapy cohort, two CD8+ T cell subpopulations were respectively identified in pre- and postchemoimmunotherapy samples, potentially linked to treatment response. Decoder-FFPE-seq establishes a robust, high-resolution, and accessible framework for sensitive ST profiling of archival tissues in fundamental and translational studies.

### [C] Senescence-modulating nanoparticles SELECT the right senescent cells to treat fibrosis and cancer.

- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42961778/

#### 摘要

Cellular senescence contributes dynamically to fibrosis and cancer depending on the senescent cell type and its tissue remodeling senescence-associated secretory phenotype; however, current senotherapies lack specificity in targeting pathogenic senescent cells and have limiting toxicities. In a recent issue of Science, Hinterleitner and colleagues designed P-selectin-directed nanoparticles (SMNPs) capable of delivering senotherapies specifically to pathogenic P-selectin+ senescent macrophages in fibrotic tissues while preventing uptake in beneficial senescent endothelial cells. Precision targeting by SMNPs resolved fibrosis and restored organ function in the lung and liver and minimized senotherapeutic toxicity in mice. In tumor models, SMNP targeting of this small senescent macrophage subset was sufficient to overcome fibrosis-mediated immune exclusion and sensitize liver and lung cancers to T cell-directed immunotherapy. P-selectin+ macrophages with senescent features were also enriched in fibrotic human tissues and treatment-refractory cancer patient samples. Overall, this study provides a platform for reducing the toxicity and enhancing the specificity of senotherapies necessary to advance their clinical development for treating chronic disease.

### [C] Exploratory transcriptomic and network pharmacology prioritization of candidate targets and phytochemicals in vascular cognitive impairment.

- PMID: 42961644

- 期刊: Naunyn-Schmiedeberg's archives of pharmacology
- 作者: Alshabrmi FM
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 30
- DOI: 10.1007/s00210-026-06042-w
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42961644/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

Vascular cognitive impairment (VCI) is closely associated with cerebral small vessel disease and is linked to increased disability, institutionalization and mortality, highlighting the need to identify biologically relevant therapeutic candidates. This study applied an exploratory transcriptomic and network pharmacology framework to prioritize candidate targets and phytochemicals relevant to VCI-associated molecular dysfunction. Analysis of the GSE201482 whole-blood dataset identified 1163 nominally differentially expressed genes between VCI and SCD/SVD comparator samples. Weighted gene co-expression network analysis identified trend-associated turquoise and green modules, yielding 352 overlapping DEG--WGCNA candidate genes. Integration with medicinal plant-derived compound targets identified 52 common candidate genes. Functional enrichment linked these genes to membrane-potential regulation, synaptic compartments, G-protein-coupled receptor signalling, oxidative stress response, hypoxia response, neuroactive ligand signalling and amino-acid metabolism. Immune deconvolution provided exploratory immune-context information on macrophage, monocyte, NK cell and T cell populations. Machine-learning feature prioritization using LASSO, Random Forest and SVM-RFE identified ACP1, CDC25A and MAO-B as candidate genes selected by all three algorithms. External assessment using the independent GSE303449 human brain RNA-seq dataset added a validation layer for candidate interpretation, although FDR-level support for the 52-gene signature and ML-derived panels was limited. MAO-B was selected for docking because it is a druggable neuroactive enzyme with established inhibitor biology and an experimentally resolved ligand-bound structure, enabling reference-based comparison with rasagiline. Docking analysis highlighted isorhamnetin, quercetin and oroxylin A as top-ranked phytochemicals against MAO-B. Overall, this study provides a reproducible candidate-prioritization workflow for guiding future experimental evaluation of multi-target phytochemical approaches in VCI.

### [C] Neuroinflammatory and synaptic mechanisms underlying cisplatin neurotoxicity in the cerebrum and hippocampus: roles of oxidative stress and BDNF, NMDAR2B, p-Tau, and Akt/GSK3β signaling.

- PMID: 42961474
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 期刊: Experimental brain research
- 作者: Altunkaya M, Abuşoğlu G, Öztürk B
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 30
- DOI: 10.1007/s00221-026-07427-2
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42961474/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

This study investigated the neurotoxic effects of cisplatin on rat cerebrum and hippocampus tissues, focusing on neuroinflammation, oxidative stress, synaptic plasticity, and neurodegeneration. Wistar albino rats were divided into three groups: a control group, a 7.5 mg/kg cisplatin group, and a 12 mg/kg cisplatin group. The control group received isotonic saline, while the treatment groups received a single intraperitoneal dose of cisplatin. At the end of the experiment, cerebrum and hippocampus tissues were collected. Oxidative stress markers (malondialdehyde [MDA], superoxide dismutase [SOD]), inflammatory cytokines (tumor necrosis factor-alpha [TNF-α], interleukin-1beta [IL-1β], interleukin-6 [IL-6], interleukin-10 [IL-10]), and neurodegenerative markers (phosphorylated-tau [p-Tau], brain-derived neurotrophic factor [BDNF], cortisol, α-amino-3-hydroxy-5-methyl-4-isoxazolepropionic acid receptor 1 [AMPA1]) were measured by enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA), whereas protein kinase B (Akt1)/glycogen synthase kinase-3 beta (GSK3β) and N-methyl-D-aspartate receptor subunit 2B (NMDAR2B) protein expression levels were analyzed by Western blot. Cisplatin administration caused a significant, dose-dependent increase in pro-inflammatory cytokines (TNF-α and IL-1β; p < 0.001) and a compensatory rise in IL-10 levels (p < 0.001) in both tissues. Oxidative stress markers, particularly MDA and SOD, were significantly elevated at the higher dose (p < 0.05). In contrast, BDNF levels (p < 0.001) and NMDAR2B expression (p < 0.05), which are essential for neuronal survival and synaptic plasticity, were markedly reduced. Additionally, p-Tau levels (p < 0.005 in the cerebrum, p < 0.002 in the hippocampus) and cortisol levels significantly increased in the 12 mg/kg group, indicating enhanced neurodegenerative processes. The hippocampus was more sensitive to cisplatin toxicity than the cerebrum, exhibiting significantly elevated IL-6 and SOD levels (p < 0.05) even at the lower 7.5 mg/kg dose. Overall, the findings demonstrate that cisplatin induces neuroinflammation, oxidative damage, and synaptic dysfunction, leading to activation of neurodegenerative pathways. This heightened vulnerability of the hippocampus may contribute to chemotherapy-associated cognitive impairment.

### [C] Understanding the roles of interleukin-33: is there promise for therapeutic targeting?

- PMID: 42961111
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology

- 期刊: Expert opinion on therapeutic targets
- 作者: Gao X, Yu Y, Zhong S, Zhang W, Li D
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 30
- DOI: 10.1080/14728222.2026.2755129
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42961111/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

INTRODUCTION: IL-33 is a chromatin-associated alarmin of the IL-1 cytokine family involved in tissue homeostasis and immune responses following epithelial or endothelial injury. Although the IL-33/ST2 axis is an attractive therapeutic target, clinical outcomes with IL-33- or ST2-directed agents have varied across diseases and patient populations.
AREAS COVERED: This review examines IL-33 biology and the clinical development of tozorakimab, itepekimab, astegolimab and emerging multispecific antibodies across COPD, asthma, atopic dermatitis, food allergy, diabetic kidney disease and viral pneumonia. Particular attention is given to IL-33 oxidation, RAGE/EGFR-associated signaling, soluble ST2, tissue compartment, therapeutic modality and trial design, and to the distinction between pathway activation and pathogenic dependence.
EXPERT OPINION: IL-33 remains a promising but context-dependent therapeutic target. Phase 3 COPD trials provide the strongest evidence, particularly for tozorakimab, whereas outcomes with other agents and indications remain inconsistent. Future development should define disease- and endotype-specific dependence on IL-33, clarify the relevance of ST2-independent signaling and soluble ST2, and validate predictive biomarkers using form-resolved assays. Structural differences among anti-IL-33 antibodies may also influence activity, but comparative clinical evidence is lacking. Multispecific approaches may broaden efficacy, but their advantages over established biologics remain to be demonstrated and require prospective evaluation in biomarker-defined patient populations.

### [C] Glymphatic Dysfunction as a Multisystem Pathway Linking Systemic Health Conditions to Alzheimer's Disease: Mechanisms, Risk Factors, and Therapeutic Opportunities.

- PMID: 42957838
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Molecular neurobiology
- 作者: Abaresh S, Ivantsova E, Parmar MS
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 30
- DOI: 10.1007/s12035-026-06297-7
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42957838/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

The glymphatic system is a glia-associated perivascular pathway involved in cerebrospinal fluid (CSF)-interstitial fluid (ISF) exchange and the transport and clearance of neurotoxic metabolites, including amyloid-β (Aβ) and tau, from the central nervous system. Growing evidence suggests that alterations in glymphatic and perivascular clearance are associated with Alzheimer's disease (AD) pathology and may contribute to the accumulation of neurotoxic proteins and neurodegeneration. This review examines the role of the glymphatic system in AD and explores how aging, sleep disturbances, hypertension, ischemic heart disease, high-fat diet, diabetes, and gut microbiome dysbiosis impair glymphatic transport. A recurring mechanism across experimental models is the loss of perivascular AQP4 polarization, while vascular, metabolic, and inflammatory conditions may converge on related perivascular and neurovascular processes that impair CSF-ISF exchange and solute handling. Neuroimaging studies using markers such as DTI-ALPS, perivascular space burden, and free-water measurements provide evidence of alterations in glymphatic-related and perivascular processes in AD and demonstrate associations with amyloid deposition, neurodegeneration, and cognitive decline. Furthermore, glymphatic dysfunction may interact with neuroinflammation and tau pathology, creating a self-reinforcing cycle that accelerates disease progression. Emerging therapeutic strategies, including continuous theta-burst stimulation, positive airway pressure therapy for obstructive sleep apnea, pharmacological enhancement of AQP4 function, and nutritional interventions, have shown potential in experimental and early translational studies to influence glymphatic-related processes and may mitigate AD-related pathology. Collectively, current findings suggest that glymphatic and perivascular clearance pathways may represent an important link between systemic health conditions and AD. Because related pathways are implicated across multiple systemic conditions, interventions targeting glymphatic and perivascular dysfunction may have broader relevance to brain health, but their clinical effects remain to be established.

### [C] Artificial intelligence applications in microbiota-gut-brain axis research: Implications for Alzheimer's disease.

- PMID: 42957639

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗

- 期刊: Journal of Alzheimer's disease : JAD
- 作者: Lista S, Emanuele E, Cuperlovic-Culf M, Mahdi A, Singh V, López-Ortiz S, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 29
- DOI: 10.1177/13872877261500124
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42957639/
- 日报日期: 2026-10-31

#### 摘要

Alterations in the gut microbiota (GM) are increasingly implicated in Alzheimer's disease (AD) pathogenesis through the microbiota-gut-brain axis (MGBA), influencing neuroinflammation, amyloid-β deposition, and tau aggregation. The inherent complexity of microbiota data necessitates artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) to identify novel biomarkers and therapeutic targets. In this narrative review, we examined AI applications in AD-related GM research, including metataxonomic and metagenomic approaches. AI methods facilitate microbial signature identification and enterotype characterization associated with AD progression, enable multi-omics integration clarifying molecular pathways linking GM dysbiosis to neurodegeneration, support predictive modeling for early AD detection, and inform personalized MGBA-targeted interventions. Employed techniques include classical ML (e.g., random forests) for taxonomic classification, deep learning (e.g., neural networks) for complex pattern recognition, and explainable AI for identifying key bacterial taxa and metabolic functions. Significant challenges persist regarding methodological standardization, larger longitudinal cohorts, and ethical considerations in clinical applications. Future priorities include developing interpretable models, integrating longitudinal multi-omics data to establish causality, and validating AI-derived insights for precision AD prevention and treatment. AI uniquely enables high-dimensional, non-linear analyses that advance understanding of microbiome-brain interactions, though clinical translation requires rigorous validation and interdisciplinary collaboration.

### [C] Ixodes ricinus-Contact Phase Inhibitor Promotes Functional Recovery and Attenuates Neutrophil Associated Neuroinflammation After Experimental Intracerebral Hemorrhage.

- PMID: 42963926
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: CNS neuroscience & therapeutics
- 作者: Demoulin S, Letourneur A, Lebrun F, Violle N, Vivien D, Pireaux V
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1002/cns.71227
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42963926/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-01

#### 摘要

AIMS: Neutrophil-associated inflammation contributes to secondary brain injury after intracerebral hemorrhage (ICH). We evaluated whether Ixodes ricinus-Contact Phase Inhibitor (Ir-CPI), a selective factor XIa/XIIa inhibitor with reported thromboinflammatory modulatory properties, improves outcome after experimental ICH without worsening bleeding.
METHODS: In a collagenase-induced mouse ICH model, Ir-CPI was administered as an intravenous bolus followed by a 3-day infusion. Vehicle animals served as controls and enoxaparin as an anticoagulant comparator. Cohorts evaluated hemorrhage and lesion volumes, endothelial activation, neutrophil-associated inflammation, and longitudinal functional recovery. Ir-CPI association with human neutrophils was evaluated in vitro.
RESULTS: Ir-CPI did not increase hemorrhage or lesion volumes versus PBS through Day 14, whereas enoxaparin showed a higher Day 1 hemorrhage volume than Ir-CPI. Flow cytometry showed Ir-CPI association with human neutrophils. In the mouse model, Ir-CPI produced region-specific reductions in neutrophil infiltration and NET formation and reduced neuronal degeneration at the injection site and caudal level, without affecting VCAM-1-associated endothelial activation. Ir-CPI accelerated functional recovery, improving neurological scores and sensorimotor asymmetry by Day 5, with restoration of baseline corner test performance by Day 14.
CONCLUSION: Ir-CPI improves recovery after experimental ICH without exacerbating bleeding and is associated with reduced neutrophil-associated secondary brain injury. These findings support its translational potential as a therapeutic strategy for ICH.

- 作者: Hübner J, McFleder R, Ip CW, Alb M, Einsele H, Hudecek M, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1002/cpz1.70503
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42961140/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-01

#### 摘要

Glioblastoma (GBM) is the most lethal primary brain tumor in adults, and despite intensive research efforts, effective therapeutic breakthroughs remain elusive. Consequently, the standard of care has remained largely unchanged for nearly 30 years, underscoring the need for robust preclinical models to evaluate experimental therapies. One particularly promising approach is chimeric antigen receptor (CAR) T cell therapy, which has demonstrated remarkable success in several hematologic malignancies but has not yet achieved comparable efficacy in solid tumors. This article describes the generation of orthotopic GBM xenografts in NSG mice, including anesthesia, surgical procedures, and postoperative care, followed by locoregional delivery of human CAR T cells into the lateral ventricle and subsequent assessment of therapeutic efficacy. Together, these protocols provide a reliable platform for evaluating GBM-directed CAR T cell therapies in both basic and translational research. © 2026 The Author(s). Current Protocols published by Wiley Periodicals LLC. Basic Protocol 1: Intracranial tumor implantation Basic Protocol 2: Intraventricular CAR T cell administration Support Protocol 1: Bioluminescence imaging of xenografts Support Protocol 2: Endpoint analysis of residual tumor burden by flow cytometry.

### [C] Sustained Tocilizumab and Adjunctive HydroBox Therapy in Refractory Takayasu Arteritis: An 11-year Case Report.

- PMID: 42957267
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: In vivo (Athens, Greece)
- 作者: Liao CY, Lu JW, Ho YJ, Lui SW, Hsieh TY, Jheng WL, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.21873/invivo.14530
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42957267/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-01

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND/AIM: Takayasu arteritis (TAK) is a rare, chronic granulomatous large-vessel vasculitis predominantly affecting young women, characterized by progressive stenosis and occlusion of the aorta and its major branches. Despite advances in biologic therapy, refractory disease with recurrent vascular complications remains a significant clinical challenge. Molecular hydrogen-based adjuvant (HydroBox) therapy has emerged as a promising adjuvant approach owing to its antioxidant and immunomodulatory properties. This report aims to describe the long-term clinical course and therapeutic outcomes of a patient with refractory TAK managed with sustained tocilizumab and HydroBox-assisted immunomodulatory therapy over 11 years.
CASE REPORT: We report the case of a 25-year-old female with refractory TAK first diagnosed at age 13, presenting with sudden vision loss, dizziness, and pulseless upper extremities. Disease course was complicated by extensive great-vessel involvement, multiple cerebral infarctions, and recurrent in-stent restenosis despite ongoing interleukin-6 (IL-6) blockade. Over 50 courses of tocilizumab (Actemra) were administered from 2014 onward, combined with multiple percutaneous transluminal angioplasty and stenting procedures. HydroBox-assisted immunomodulatory therapy was subsequently introduced as an adjunctive treatment. During continued follow-up, the patient remained clinically stable with sustained suppression of inflammatory markers and favorable cerebrovascular findings on serial imaging.
CONCLUSION: This 11-year case report documents the feasibility of sustained long-term tocilizumab therapy combined with adjunctive HydroBox therapy in a patient with refractory TAK. Despite extensive multi-vessel involvement, recurrent in-stent restenosis, and prior cerebrovascular ischemia, the patient achieved progressive hemodynamic stabilization, durable suppression of inflammatory markers, and continued disease control. The potential immunomodulatory and antioxidant properties of HydroBox-assisted therapy warrant further prospective investigation to establish standardized protocols and confirm efficacy in larger cohorts.

### [C] Renal and Vascular Effects of Selective FGFR4 Inhibition in Folic Acid-induced Kidney Injury in Rats.

- PMID: 42957224
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: In vivo (Athens, Greece)
- 作者: Rumanli Z, Avci E, Yilmaz ÜI, Baykal B, Yildirim BK, Aksoy OA, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.21873/invivo.14498
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42957224/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-01

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND/AIM: Fibroblast growth factor-23 (FGF-23) has been implicated in chronic kidney disease (CKD)-associated cardiovascular dysfunction, potentially through fibroblast growth factor receptor 4 (FGFR4). This study investigated renal and vascular alterations in folic acid-induced CKD and evaluated the effects of selective FGFR4 inhibition.
MATERIALS AND METHODS: Male Sprague-Dawley rats were assigned to healthy control, CKD control, vehicle, and roblitinib-treated groups. CKD was induced by a single intraperitoneal dose of folic acid. Serum creatinine, FGF-23, tumor necrosis factor-α, interleukin-1β, and asymmetric dimethylarginine levels were measured. Vascular reactivity was evaluated in isolated aortic rings using phenylephrine, carbachol, and sodium nitroprusside. Renal fibrosis, carotid calcification, and medial thickness were assessed histologically.
RESULTS: Folic acid administration increased serum creatinine and relative kidney weight and impaired both endothelium-dependent and endothelium-independent vascular relaxation. Relative kidney weight was lower in the roblitinib-treated group than in the CKD control group. However, changes in vascular responses observed in both the treatment and vehicle groups prevented these effects from being attributed specifically to FGFR4 inhibition. FGF-23 and inflammatory marker levels did not differ significantly among the groups. Carotid medial thickness was significantly greater in the treatment group than in the CKD-control group (p<0.05), whereas no other significant between-group differences were detected.
CONCLUSION: Folic acid-induced renal injury was associated with early functional vascular impairment despite limited structural and inflammatory alterations. Selective FGFR4 inhibition did not demonstrate a clear therapeutic effect. Further in vivo studies using complementary CKD models, direct assessment of FGFR4 signaling, and vehicle formulations without intrinsic vascular activity are required to validate these findings.

### [C] Inhibition of VEGFR1 Tyrosine Kinase Signaling Attenuates Development of Lung Emphysema by Recruiting Monocyte-derived Macrophages.

- PMID: 42957217
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory

- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: In vivo (Athens, Greece)
- 作者: Akinaga S, Ito Y, Osada M, Yamashita A, Yamazaki T, Hokama S, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.21873/invivo.14486
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42957217/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-01

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND/AIM: Macrophages are pivotal in the early stages of emphysema development within the lungs. Although vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF) is implicated in emphysema formation, the contribution of signaling by VEGF receptor 1 (VEGFR1), which is highly expressed in monocytes and macrophages, to its progression remains uncertain. We explored whether VEGFR1 tyrosine kinase (TK) signaling in macrophages promoted emphysema progression and whether its inhibition mitigated disease by altering the phenotype of recruited macrophages.
MATERIALS AND METHODS: Male Vegfr1 TK-deficient (TK-/-) mice or wild-type (WT) mice were subjected to an elastase-induced lung emphysema model. We assessed airspace enlargement, inflammatory mediators, and macrophage accumulation.
RESULTS: Compared with WT mice, TK-/- mice showed attenuated emphysema development and lower levels of pro-inflammatory mediators, such as matrix metalloproteinase-12, while exhibiting increased anti-inflammatory mediators. Elastase induced a transient elevation of monocyte-derived macrophages (MDMs), peaking on day 3; this recruitment was greater in TK-/- mice than in WT mice. MDMs and bone marrow-derived macrophages from TK-/- mice demonstrated reduced pro-inflammatory and enhanced anti-inflammatory profiles. The transfer of WT-MDMs to TK-/- mice aggravated emphysema and inflammation. Conversely, WT mice treated with a VEGFR1 inhibitor exhibited attenuation of elastase-induced lung emphysema, with reduced inflammation and increased MDMs.
CONCLUSION: These results suggest that inhibiting VEGFR1 TK signaling minimizes elastase-induced emphysema development by shifting recruited MDMs toward an anti-inflammatory profile and restraining matrix metalloproteinase-12-linked proteolytic inflammation, supporting VEGFR1 TK as a candidate therapeutic axis for early emphysema.

### [C] Quercetin activates lysosomal function to ameliorate neuroinflammation and cognitive deficits in chronic cerebral hypoperfusion.

- PMID: 42951502
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Chinese journal of natural medicines
- 作者: Chen Y, Jiang Y, Song T, Chen Y, Li J, Zhi N, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1016/S1875-5364(26)61225-3
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42951502/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-01

#### 摘要

Chronic cerebral hypoperfusion (CCH) is a major contributor to vascular cognitive impairment (VCI), often resulting in progressive learning and memory deficits. Quercetin (Qu), a natural flavonoid with potent anti-inflammatory and antioxidant properties, demonstrates neuroprotective potential against ischemic injury, yet its mechanism of action remains incompletely understood. In this study, a rat model of CCH was established by bilateral common carotid artery occlusion, followed by Qu administration at doses of 20, 40, and 80 mg·kg-1. In parallel, HT22 cells subjected to oxygen-glucose deprivation (OGD) were treated with Qu at 5, 10, or 20 μmol·L-1. Behavioral tests, histological staining, ELISA, and Western blot were used to assess cognitive performance, neuronal injury, lysosomal activity, NF-κB pathway activation, and inflammatory cytokine levels. Hippocampal RNA sequencing was also performed. Our results demonstrate that Qu greatly improved cognitive performance and attenuated neuronal damage in both the cerebral cortex and hippocampus. Mechanistically, Qu improved lysosomal function and suppressed NF-κB-mediated neuroinflammatory response, leading to less production of pro-inflammatory cytokines. Notably, co-administration of a lysosomal inhibitor attenuated the neuroprotective effects of Qu, underscoring the functional importance of lysosomal activation. In conclusion, Qu alleviates CCH-induced cognitive decline and neurodegeneration by restoring lysosomal function and inhibiting NF-κB-driven neuroinflammation. These findings support the therapeutic potential of Qu for VCI and provide a rationale for its further development as a neuroprotective agent.
